# Plan: Benchmark and Optimize `entire enable` Command

## Context

The `enable_performance_optimzations` branch already has the `benchutil` package and mise.toml tasks for benchmarking. The `entire status` command was optimized separately. Now we tackle `entire enable`, focusing on startup time in a new repo.

The non-interactive path (`entire enable --agent claude-code` → `setupAgentHooksNonInteractive()`) is the benchmark target since interactive prompts can't be benchmarked deterministically.

## Identified Hot Spots

Tracing the non-interactive enable path reveals these redundant operations:

| Redundancy | Calls | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| `GetHooksDir()` → `git rev-parse --git-path hooks` | 2x (InstallGitHook + IsGitHookInstalled in EnsureSetup) | Process spawn each time |
| `settings.Load()` via `isLocalDev()` | 2-3x (InstallGitHook + CheckAndWarnHookManagers) | 2 file reads + JSON parse each |
| `OpenRepository()` → `GetWorktreePath()` → `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` | 2x (empty check in RunE + EnsureSetup) | Process spawn + go-git open each |
| `EnsureEntireGitignore()` | 2x (setupEntireDirectory + EnsureSetup) | File read + string compare each |
| `IsGitHookInstalled()` in EnsureSetup | 1x after InstallGitHook already ran | GetHooksDir exec + 4 file reads |

## Implementation

### Step 1: Create benchmark (`cmd/entire/cli/bench_enable_test.go`)

New file with benchmarks for `setupAgentHooksNonInteractive()`:
- `BenchmarkEnableCommand/NewRepo_ClaudeCode` — fresh repo, first enable
- `BenchmarkEnableCommand/ReEnable_ClaudeCode` — already-enabled repo, re-run

Uses `benchutil.NewBenchRepo()` + `os.Chdir` (not parallelizable per CLAUDE.md exceptions). Pattern: `b.StopTimer()` for setup, `b.StartTimer()` for the measured call, `paths.ClearRepoRootCache()` between iterations.

### Step 2: Cache `GetHooksDir()` result

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/hooks.go`

Add a CWD-keyed cache (same pattern as `paths.RepoRoot()`). Add `ClearHooksDirCache()` for tests. The hooks dir doesn't change during a single CLI invocation.

### Step 3: Make `OpenRepository()` use `paths.RepoRoot()`

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/common.go:560`

Replace `GetWorktreePath()` with `paths.RepoRoot()` — same `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` but cached. By the time `OpenRepository()` runs in the enable flow, `paths.RepoRoot()` cache is already warm from `setup.go:71`.

### Step 4: Pass `localDev` to `InstallGitHook()` and `CheckAndWarnHookManagers()`

**Files:** `strategy/hooks.go`, `strategy/hook_managers.go`, `setup.go`, `manual_commit.go`, `auto_commit.go`

Currently `InstallGitHook()` calls `hookCmdPrefix()` → `isLocalDev()` → `settings.Load()` which re-reads 2 JSON files. The caller already knows `localDev`. Add a `localDev` parameter to eliminate the redundant reads.

Same for `CheckAndWarnHookManagers()` which also calls `hookCmdPrefix()`.

Update all callers (5-6 call sites in setup.go, manual_commit.go, auto_commit.go).

### Step 5: Eliminate redundant work in `EnsureSetup()`

**Files:** `strategy/manual_commit.go`, `strategy/auto_commit.go`, `setup.go`

Add `EnsureSetupWithOpts(opts)` alongside the existing parameterless `EnsureSetup()` (preserves backward compat, no interface change):

```go
type EnsureSetupOpts struct {
    SkipGitignore bool            // Already called EnsureEntireGitignore
    SkipHookCheck bool            // Already installed hooks
    Repo          *git.Repository // Reuse already-opened repo
    LocalDev      bool            // For InstallGitHook fallback
}
```

In `setup.go`, the enable paths pass:
- `SkipGitignore: true` (setupEntireDirectory already called it)
- `SkipHookCheck: true` (InstallGitHook already ran)
- `Repo: repo` (from the empty-repo check, when available)

### Step 6: Add E2E wall-clock timing script (`mise run bench:enable`)

**File:** `mise.toml` — new task `bench:enable`

A shell script that measures real wall-clock time of the full compiled `entire enable --agent claude-code` binary in a fresh git repo. This captures the true end-to-end startup time including process launch, Go runtime init, and all I/O — not just the Go function internals.

The script:
1. Builds the `entire` binary once via `mise run build`
2. Creates a temp git repo with an initial commit
3. Runs `entire enable --agent claude-code --telemetry=false` N times (default 10), capturing wall-clock time via Go's `time` or the built-in `time` command
4. Reports min/median/max/mean times
5. Cleans up

This is non-deterministic but gives a clear before/after signal. Run it on main first, note the numbers, then run on the feature branch.

```toml
[tasks."bench:enable"]
description = "Measure wall-clock time of 'entire enable' in a fresh repo"
run = """
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

RUNS="${BENCH_RUNS:-10}"

echo "=== Building entire binary ==="
mise run build

ENTIRE="$(pwd)/entire"
if [ ! -x "$ENTIRE" ]; then
  echo "Build failed — binary not found"
  exit 1
fi

tmpdir=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$tmpdir"' EXIT

times=()
for i in $(seq 1 "$RUNS"); do
  # Fresh repo each run
  dir="$tmpdir/repo-$i"
  mkdir -p "$dir"
  git -C "$dir" init -q
  git -C "$dir" commit --allow-empty -m "init" -q

  # Measure wall-clock time (milliseconds)
  start=$(python3 -c 'import time; print(int(time.time()*1000))')
  (cd "$dir" && "$ENTIRE" enable --agent claude-code --telemetry=false) >/dev/null 2>&1
  end=$(python3 -c 'import time; print(int(time.time()*1000))')
  elapsed=$((end - start))
  times+=("$elapsed")
  printf "  Run %2d: %d ms\n" "$i" "$elapsed"
done

# Stats via python
printf "\n=== Results (%d runs) ===\n" "$RUNS"
python3 -c "
import statistics
t = [$(IFS=,; echo "${times[*]}")]
print(f'  Min:    {min(t)} ms')
print(f'  Median: {int(statistics.median(t))} ms')
print(f'  Mean:   {int(statistics.mean(t))} ms')
print(f'  Max:    {max(t)} ms')
print(f'  Stdev:  {int(statistics.stdev(t))} ms')
"
"""
```

Usage: Run on main first to get a baseline, then on the feature branch to compare.

```bash
# On main
git stash && git checkout main
mise run bench:enable    # note the median
git checkout - && git stash pop

# On feature branch (after optimizations)
mise run bench:enable    # compare median
```

### Step 7: Verify against main

```bash
# Go benchmarks (deterministic, statistical comparison)
BENCH_PATTERN=BenchmarkEnableCommand mise run bench:compare

# E2E wall-clock (real binary, non-deterministic but shows real-world impact)
mise run bench:enable
```

## 1. `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/hooks.go`

### `GetHooksDir()` (line 46-48)
```go
func GetHooksDir() (string, error) {
	return getHooksDirInPath(".")
}
```
Delegates to `getHooksDirInPath(".")` which runs `git rev-parse --git-path hooks` (a child process spawn for every call). Returns the active hooks directory respecting `core.hooksPath` and linked worktrees.

### `getHooksDirInPath(dir string)` (line 76-91)
```go
func getHooksDirInPath(dir string) (string, error) {
	ctx := context.Background()
	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--git-path", "hooks")
	cmd.Dir = dir
	output, err := cmd.Output()
	if err != nil {
		return "", errors.New("not a git repository")
	}
	hooksDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
	if !filepath.IsAbs(hooksDir) {
		hooksDir = filepath.Join(dir, hooksDir)
	}
	return filepath.Clean(hooksDir), nil
}
```
**Key note**: Spawns a `git` child process every invocation. No caching.

### `InstallGitHook(silent bool) (int, error)` (line 169-222)

		hookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, spec.name)
		// ... backup logic, chain logic, writeHookFile()
	}
	if !silent { /* print */ }
	return installedCount, nil
}
```
Calls `GetHooksDir()` (1 subprocess), then `hookCmdPrefix()` which calls `isLocalDev()` which calls `settings.Load()`. Then iterates 4 hooks, reading/writing each.

func CheckAndWarnHookManagers(w io.Writer) {
	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
	if err != nil {
		return
	}
	managers := detectHookManagers(repoRoot)
	if len(managers) == 0 {
		return
	}
	warning := hookManagerWarning(managers, hookCmdPrefix())
	if warning != "" {
		fmt.Fprintln(w)
		fmt.Fprint(w, warning)
	}
}
```
Calls `paths.RepoRoot()` (cached by CWD), then `detectHookManagers()` which does filesystem-only checks (up to ~20 `os.Stat` calls for lefthook variants + 3 other checks). Then calls `hookCmdPrefix()` which again calls `isLocalDev()` -> `settings.Load()`.

### `detectHookManagers(repoRoot string) []hookManager` (line 22-54)
Builds a list of up to ~19 `hookManager` checks (Husky, pre-commit, Overcommit, plus 16 Lefthook variants), does `os.Stat()` on each. Returns matches (deduped by name).

## 3. `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/common.go`

### `OpenRepository() (*git.Repository, error)` (line 560-577)
```go
func OpenRepository() (*git.Repository, error) {
	repoRoot, err := GetWorktreePath()
	if err != nil {
		repoRoot = "."
	}
	repo, err := git.PlainOpenWithOptions(repoRoot, &git.PlainOpenOptions{
		EnableDotGitCommonDir: true,
	})
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open repository: %w", err)
	}
	return repo, nil
}
```
**Key note**: Calls `GetWorktreePath()` which spawns `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` every time. Then opens the repo with go-git. **No caching** -- every caller gets a new `git.Repository`. This is called extremely frequently throughout the codebase.

### `GetWorktreePath() (string, error)` (line 661-669)
```go
func GetWorktreePath() (string, error) {
	ctx := context.Background()
	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel")
	output, err := cmd.Output()
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get worktree path: %w", err)
	}
	return strings.TrimSpace(string(output)), nil
}
```
**No caching**. Spawns a git subprocess every call. Compare with `paths.RepoRoot()` which does the same thing but with caching.

### `EnsureEntireGitignore()` (line 673-723)
Reads `.entire/.gitignore`, checks for 4 required entries (`tmp/`, `settings.local.json`, `metadata/`, `logs/`), appends missing ones. Calls `paths.AbsPath()` which calls `paths.RepoRoot()` (cached).

### `EnsureMetadataBranch(repo *git.Repository) error` (line 261-316)
Checks if `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch exists. If not, creates an orphan commit with empty tree and creates the branch reference. Prints to stderr on creation.

## 4. `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/setup.go`

### `EnsureSetup()` is not a standalone function -- it's called on strategy objects. The setup flow is in these key functions:

### `setupAgentHooksNonInteractive(w io.Writer, ag agent.Agent, strategyName string, localDev, forceHooks, skipPushSessions, telemetry bool) error` (line 707-806)
The non-interactive `entire enable --agent` path. Order of operations:
1. `hookAgent.InstallHooks(localDev, forceHooks)` -- install agent-specific hooks
2. `setupEntireDirectory()` -- create `.entire/` and gitignore
3. `LoadEntireSettings()` -- load existing settings
4. Update settings fields (enabled, strategy, localDev, push_sessions, telemetry)
5. `SaveEntireSettings(settings)` -- write settings
6. `strategy.InstallGitHook(true)` -- install 4 git hooks (spawns `git rev-parse --git-path hooks`)
7. `strategy.CheckAndWarnHookManagers(w)` -- detect hook managers (more filesystem checks)
8. `strategy.Get(settings.Strategy)` -- get strategy instance
9. `strat.EnsureSetup()` -- strategy-specific setup (creates metadata branch, etc.)

### `setupEntireDirectory() (bool, error)` (line 844-869)
```go
func setupEntireDirectory() (bool, error) {
	entireDirAbs, err := paths.AbsPath(paths.EntireDir)
	if err != nil {
		entireDirAbs = paths.EntireDir
	}
	created := false
	if _, err := os.Stat(entireDirAbs); os.IsNotExist(err) {
		created = true
	}
	if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDirAbs, 0o755); err != nil {
		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to create .entire directory: %w", err)
	}
	if err := strategy.EnsureEntireGitignore(); err != nil {
		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to setup .gitignore: %w", err)
	}
	return created, nil
}
```
Calls `paths.AbsPath()` (which calls `RepoRoot()`), then `os.MkdirAll()`, then `strategy.EnsureEntireGitignore()` (which also calls `paths.AbsPath()`).

### `runEnableWithStrategy(...)` (line 188-284) and `runEnableInteractive(...)` (line 288-389)
Both follow the same pattern:
1. Uninstall deselected agent hooks
2. Setup agent hooks for each selected agent
3. `setupEntireDirectory()`
4. Load/save settings
5. `strategy.InstallGitHook(true)`
6. `strategy.CheckAndWarnHookManagers(w)`
7. `strat.EnsureSetup()`

### `ManualCommitStrategy.EnsureSetup() error` (line 99-120)
```go
func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) EnsureSetup() error {
	if err := EnsureEntireGitignore(); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	repo, err := OpenRepository()
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("failed to open git repository: %w", err)
	}
	if err := EnsureMetadataBranch(repo); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("failed to ensure metadata branch: %w", err)
	}
	if !IsGitHookInstalled() {
		if _, err := InstallGitHook(true); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("failed to install git hooks: %w", err)
		}
	}
	return nil
}
```
Three steps:
1. `EnsureEntireGitignore()` -- reads/writes `.entire/.gitignore`
2. `OpenRepository()` then `EnsureMetadataBranch(repo)` -- creates orphan branch if needed
3. `IsGitHookInstalled()` -- calls `GetHooksDir()` (subprocess) then reads 4 hook files. If not installed, calls `InstallGitHook(true)`.

**Key observation**: `IsGitHookInstalled()` calls `GetHooksDir()` which spawns a subprocess. Then `InstallGitHook()` calls `GetHooksDir()` again. This is redundant.

## 7. `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths.go`

### `RepoRoot() (string, error)` (line 61-93)
```go
var (
	repoRootMu       sync.RWMutex
	repoRootCache    string
	repoRootCacheDir string
)

func RepoRoot() (string, error) {
	cwd, err := os.Getwd()
	if err != nil {
		cwd = ""
	}
	repoRootMu.RLock()
	if repoRootCache != "" && repoRootCacheDir == cwd {
		cached := repoRootCache
		repoRootMu.RUnlock()
		return cached, nil
	}
	repoRootMu.RUnlock()
	// Cache miss
	ctx := context.Background()
	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel")
	output, err := cmd.Output()
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get git repository root: %w", err)
	}
	root := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
	repoRootMu.Lock()
	repoRootCache = root
	repoRootCacheDir = cwd
	repoRootMu.Unlock()
	return root, nil
}
```
**Has RWMutex caching** keyed by CWD. First call spawns a subprocess, subsequent calls from the same CWD return from cache. `ClearRepoRootCache()` (line 97-102) resets it.

**Key contrast**: `strategy.GetWorktreePath()` does the exact same `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` but has **no caching**.

## Summary of Performance Optimization Opportunities

Based on this analysis, the `entire enable` command has several performance bottlenecks:

1. **Redundant subprocess spawning**: `GetWorktreePath()` (uncached) vs `paths.RepoRoot()` (cached) -- both call `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`. `OpenRepository()` calls `GetWorktreePath()` on every invocation with no caching.

2. **Duplicate `GetHooksDir()` calls**: `IsGitHookInstalled()` calls `GetHooksDir()`, and if hooks aren't installed, `InstallGitHook()` calls it again. Each call spawns `git rev-parse --git-path hooks`.

3. **Duplicate `EnsureEntireGitignore()`**: Called in `setupEntireDirectory()` AND again inside `ManualCommitStrategy.EnsureSetup()` / `AutoCommitStrategy.EnsureSetup()`.

4. **Duplicate `InstallGitHook()`**: Called in `setupAgentHooksNonInteractive()` / `runEnableWithStrategy()` AND again inside `EnsureSetup()` on each strategy.

5. **Multiple `settings.Load()`**: `hookCmdPrefix()` -> `isLocalDev()` -> `settings.Load()` is called from both `InstallGitHook()` and `CheckAndWarnHookManagers()`.

6. **Multiple `OpenRepository()` calls**: The enable flow calls it at least 3 times (validation, strategy.EnsureSetup, plus `EnsureMetadataBranch`).

7. **`EnsureSetup()` is identical** for both strategies -- candidate for extraction to shared implementation.

    14→
    15→// Hook marker used to identify Entire CLI hooks
    16→const entireHookMarker = "Entire CLI hooks"
    17→
    18→const backupSuffix = ".pre-entire"
    19→const chainComment = "# Chain: run pre-existing hook"
    20→
    21→// gitHookNames are the git hooks managed by Entire CLI

    23→
    24→// ManagedGitHookNames returns the list of git hooks managed by Entire CLI.
    25→// This is useful for tests that need to manipulate hooks.
    26→func ManagedGitHookNames() []string {
    27→	return gitHookNames
    28→}
    29→
    30→// hookSpec defines a git hook's name and content template (without chain call).
    31→type hookSpec struct {
    32→	name    string
    33→	content string
    34→}
    35→
    36→// GetGitDir returns the actual git directory path by delegating to git itself.
    37→// This handles both regular repositories and worktrees, and inherits git's
    38→// security validation for gitdir references.
    39→func GetGitDir() (string, error) {
    40→	return getGitDirInPath(".")
    41→}
    42→
    43→// GetHooksDir returns the active hooks directory path.
    44→// This respects core.hooksPath and correctly resolves to the common hooks
    45→// directory when called from a linked worktree.
    46→func GetHooksDir() (string, error) {
    47→	return getHooksDirInPath(".")
    48→}
    49→
    50→// getGitDirInPath returns the git directory for a repository at the given path.
    51→// It delegates to `git rev-parse --git-dir` to leverage git's own validation.
    52→func getGitDirInPath(dir string) (string, error) {
    53→	ctx := context.Background()
    54→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--git-dir")
    55→	cmd.Dir = dir
    56→	output, err := cmd.Output()
    57→	if err != nil {
    58→		return "", errors.New("not a git repository")
    59→	}
    60→
    61→	gitDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
    62→
    63→	// git rev-parse --git-dir returns relative paths from the working directory,
    64→	// so we need to make it absolute if it isn't already
    65→	if !filepath.IsAbs(gitDir) {
    66→		gitDir = filepath.Join(dir, gitDir)
    67→	}
    68→
    69→	return filepath.Clean(gitDir), nil
    70→}
    71→
    72→// getHooksDirInPath returns the active hooks directory for a repository at the given path.
    73→// It delegates to `git rev-parse --git-path hooks` so Git resolves:
    74→// - linked-worktree common hooks directory
    75→// - core.hooksPath (relative or absolute)
    76→func getHooksDirInPath(dir string) (string, error) {
    77→	ctx := context.Background()
    78→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--git-path", "hooks")
    79→	cmd.Dir = dir
    80→	output, err := cmd.Output()
    81→	if err != nil {
    82→		return "", errors.New("not a git repository")
    83→	}
    84→
    85→	hooksDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
    86→	if !filepath.IsAbs(hooksDir) {
    87→		hooksDir = filepath.Join(dir, hooksDir)
    88→	}
    89→
    90→	return filepath.Clean(hooksDir), nil
    91→}

    93→// IsGitHookInstalled checks if all generic Entire CLI hooks are installed.

    96→	if err != nil {
    97→		return false
    98→	}
    99→	return isGitHookInstalledInHooksDir(hooksDir)
   100→}
   101→
   102→// IsGitHookInstalledInDir checks if all Entire CLI hooks are installed in the given repo directory.
   103→// This is useful for tests that need to check hooks without changing the working directory.
   104→func IsGitHookInstalledInDir(repoDir string) bool {
   105→	hooksDir, err := getHooksDirInPath(repoDir)
   106→	if err != nil {
   107→		return false
   108→	}
   109→	return isGitHookInstalledInHooksDir(hooksDir)
   110→}
   111→
   112→// isGitHookInstalledInHooksDir checks if all hooks are installed in the given hooks directory.
   113→func isGitHookInstalledInHooksDir(hooksDir string) bool {
   114→	for _, hook := range gitHookNames {
   115→		hookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hook)
   116→		data, err := os.ReadFile(hookPath) //nolint:gosec // Path is constructed from constants
   117→		if err != nil {
   118→			return false
   119→		}
   120→		if !strings.Contains(string(data), entireHookMarker) {
   121→			return false
   122→		}
   123→	}
   124→	return true
   125→}
   126→
   127→// buildHookSpecs returns the hook specifications for all managed hooks.
   128→func buildHookSpecs(cmdPrefix string) []hookSpec {
   129→	return []hookSpec{
   130→		{
   131→			name: "prepare-commit-msg",
   132→			content: fmt.Sprintf(`#!/bin/sh
   133→# %s
   134→%s hooks git prepare-commit-msg "$1" "$2" 2>/dev/null || true
   135→`, entireHookMarker, cmdPrefix),
   136→		},
   137→		{
   138→			name: "commit-msg",
   139→			content: fmt.Sprintf(`#!/bin/sh
   140→# %s
   141→# Commit-msg hook: strip trailer if no user content (allows aborting empty commits)
   142→%s hooks git commit-msg "$1" || exit 1
   143→`, entireHookMarker, cmdPrefix),
   144→		},
   145→		{
   146→			name: "post-commit",
   147→			content: fmt.Sprintf(`#!/bin/sh
   148→# %s
   149→# Post-commit hook: condense session data if commit has Entire-Checkpoint trailer
   150→%s hooks git post-commit 2>/dev/null || true
   151→`, entireHookMarker, cmdPrefix),
   152→		},
   153→		{
   154→			name: "pre-push",
   155→			content: fmt.Sprintf(`#!/bin/sh
   156→# %s
   157→# Pre-push hook: push session logs alongside user's push
   158→# $1 is the remote name (e.g., "origin")
   159→%s hooks git pre-push "$1" || true
   160→`, entireHookMarker, cmdPrefix),
   161→		},
   162→	}
   163→}
   164→
   165→// InstallGitHook installs generic git hooks that delegate to `entire hook` commands.
   166→// These hooks work with any strategy - the strategy is determined at runtime.
   167→// If silent is true, no output is printed (except backup notifications, which always print).
   168→// Returns the number of hooks that were installed (0 if all already up to date).
   169→func InstallGitHook(silent bool) (int, error) {
   170→	hooksDir, err := GetHooksDir()
   171→	if err != nil {
   172→		return 0, err
   173→	}
   174→
   175→	if err := os.MkdirAll(hooksDir, 0o755); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // Git hooks require executable permissions
   176→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to create hooks directory: %w", err)
   177→	}
   178→
   179→	specs := buildHookSpecs(hookCmdPrefix())
   180→	installedCount := 0
   181→
   182→	for _, spec := range specs {
   183→		hookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, spec.name)
   184→		backupPath := hookPath + backupSuffix
   185→		backupExists := fileExists(backupPath)
   186→
   187→		// Back up existing non-Entire hooks
   188→		existing, existingErr := os.ReadFile(hookPath) //nolint:gosec // path is controlled
   189→		if existingErr == nil && !strings.Contains(string(existing), entireHookMarker) {
   190→			if !backupExists {
   191→				if err := os.Rename(hookPath, backupPath); err != nil {
   192→					return installedCount, fmt.Errorf("failed to back up %s: %w", spec.name, err)
   193→				}
   194→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Backed up existing %s to %s%s\n", spec.name, spec.name, backupSuffix)
   195→			} else {
   196→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: replacing %s (backup %s%s already exists from a previous install)\n", spec.name, spec.name, backupSuffix)
   197→			}
   198→			backupExists = true
   199→		}
   200→
   201→		// Chain to backup if one exists
   202→		content := spec.content
   203→		if backupExists {
   204→			content = generateChainedContent(spec.content, spec.name)
   205→		}
   206→
   207→		written, err := writeHookFile(hookPath, content)
   208→		if err != nil {
   209→			return installedCount, fmt.Errorf("failed to install %s hook: %w", spec.name, err)
   210→		}
   211→		if written {
   212→			installedCount++
   213→		}
   214→	}
   215→
   216→	if !silent {
   217→		fmt.Println("✓ Installed git hooks (prepare-commit-msg, commit-msg, post-commit, pre-push)")
   218→		fmt.Println("  Hooks delegate to the current strategy at runtime")
   219→	}
   220→
   221→	return installedCount, nil
   222→}
   223→
   224→// writeHookFile writes a hook file if it doesn't exist or has different content.
   225→// Returns true if the file was written, false if it already had the same content.
   226→func writeHookFile(path, content string) (bool, error) {
   227→	// Check if file already exists with same content
   228→	existing, err := os.ReadFile(path) //nolint:gosec // path is controlled
   229→	if err == nil && string(existing) == content {
   230→		return false, nil // Already up to date
   231→	}
   232→
   233→	// Git hooks must be executable (0o755)
   234→	if err := os.WriteFile(path, []byte(content), 0o755); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // Git hooks require executable permissions
   235→		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to write hook file %s: %w", path, err)
   236→	}
   237→	return true, nil
   238→}
   239→
   240→// RemoveGitHook removes all Entire CLI git hooks from the repository.
   241→// If a .pre-entire backup exists, it is restored.
   242→// Returns the number of hooks removed.
   243→func RemoveGitHook() (int, error) {
   244→	hooksDir, err := GetHooksDir()
   245→	if err != nil {
   246→		return 0, err
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	removed := 0
   250→	var removeErrors []string
   251→
   252→	for _, hook := range gitHookNames {
   253→		hookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hook)
   254→		backupPath := hookPath + backupSuffix
   255→
   256→		// Remove the hook if it contains our marker
   257→		data, err := os.ReadFile(hookPath) //nolint:gosec // path is controlled
   258→		hookIsOurs := err == nil && strings.Contains(string(data), entireHookMarker)
   259→		hookExists := err == nil
   260→
   261→		if hookIsOurs {
   262→			if err := os.Remove(hookPath); err != nil {
   263→				removeErrors = append(removeErrors, fmt.Sprintf("%s: %v", hook, err))
   264→				continue
   265→			}
   266→			removed++
   267→		}
   268→
   269→		// Restore .pre-entire backup if it exists
   270→		if fileExists(backupPath) {
   271→			if hookExists && !hookIsOurs {
   272→				// A non-Entire hook is present — don't overwrite it with the backup
   273→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: %s was modified since install; backup %s%s left in place\n", hook, hook, backupSuffix)
   274→			} else {
   275→				if err := os.Rename(backupPath, hookPath); err != nil {
   276→					removeErrors = append(removeErrors, fmt.Sprintf("restore %s%s: %v", hook, backupSuffix, err))
   277→				}
   278→			}
   279→		}
   280→	}
   281→
   282→	if len(removeErrors) > 0 {
   283→		return removed, fmt.Errorf("failed to remove hooks: %s", strings.Join(removeErrors, "; "))
   284→	}
   285→	return removed, nil
   286→}
   287→
   288→// generateChainedContent appends a chain call to the base hook content,
   289→// so the pre-existing hook (backed up to .pre-entire) is called after our hook.
   290→func generateChainedContent(baseContent, hookName string) string {
   291→	return baseContent + fmt.Sprintf(`%s
   292→_entire_hook_dir="$(dirname "$0")"
   293→if [ -x "$_entire_hook_dir/%s%s" ]; then
   294→    "$_entire_hook_dir/%s%s" "$@"
   295→fi
   296→`, chainComment, hookName, backupSuffix, hookName, backupSuffix)
   297→}
   298→
   299→// hookCmdPrefix returns the command prefix for hook scripts and warning messages.
   300→// Returns "go run ./cmd/entire/main.go" when local_dev is enabled, "entire" otherwise.
   301→func hookCmdPrefix() string {
   302→	if isLocalDev() {
   303→		return "go run ./cmd/entire/main.go"
   304→	}
   305→	return "entire"
   306→}
   307→
   308→// isLocalDev reads the local_dev setting from .entire/settings.json
   309→// Works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository.
   310→func isLocalDev() bool {
   311→	s, err := settings.Load()
   312→	if err != nil {
   313→		return false
   314→	}
   315→	return s.LocalDev
   316→}
   317→

     1→package strategy
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"fmt"
     5→	"io"
     6→	"os"
     7→	"path/filepath"
     8→	"strings"
     9→
    10→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    11→)
    12→
    13→// hookManager describes an external hook manager detected in a repository.
    14→type hookManager struct {
    15→	Name            string // "Husky", "Lefthook", "pre-commit", "Overcommit"
    16→	ConfigPath      string // relative path that triggered detection (e.g., ".husky/")
    17→	OverwritesHooks bool   // true if the tool will overwrite Entire's hooks on reinstall
    18→}
    19→
    20→// detectHookManagers checks the repository root for known hook manager config
    21→// files/directories. Detection is filesystem-only (os.Stat, no file reads).
    22→func detectHookManagers(repoRoot string) []hookManager {
    23→	var managers []hookManager
    24→
    25→	checks := []hookManager{
    26→		{"Husky", ".husky/", true},
    27→		{"pre-commit", ".pre-commit-config.yaml", false},
    28→		{"Overcommit", ".overcommit.yml", false},
    29→	}
    30→
    31→	// Lefthook supports {.,}lefthook{,-local}.{yml,yaml,json,toml}
    32→	for _, prefix := range []string{"", "."} {
    33→		for _, variant := range []string{"", "-local"} {
    34→			for _, ext := range []string{"yml", "yaml", "json", "toml"} {
    35→				name := prefix + "lefthook" + variant + "." + ext
    36→				checks = append(checks, hookManager{"Lefthook", name, false})
    37→			}
    38→		}
    39→	}
    40→
    41→	seen := make(map[string]bool)
    42→	for _, c := range checks {
    43→		path := filepath.Join(repoRoot, c.ConfigPath)
    44→		if _, err := os.Stat(path); err == nil {
    45→			if seen[c.Name] {
    46→				continue // e.g., lefthook.yml and .lefthook.yml both present
    47→			}
    48→			seen[c.Name] = true
    49→			managers = append(managers, c)
    50→		}
    51→	}
    52→
    53→	return managers
    54→}
    55→
    56→// hookManagerWarning builds a warning string for detected hook managers.
    57→// cmdPrefix is the CLI command prefix (e.g., "entire" or "go run ./cmd/entire/main.go").
    58→func hookManagerWarning(managers []hookManager, cmdPrefix string) string {
    59→	if len(managers) == 0 {
    60→		return ""
    61→	}
    62→
    63→	var b strings.Builder
    64→
    65→	specs := buildHookSpecs(cmdPrefix)
    66→
    67→	for _, m := range managers {
    68→		if m.OverwritesHooks {
    69→			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "Warning: %s detected (%s)\n", m.Name, m.ConfigPath)
    70→			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "\n")
    71→			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "  %s may overwrite hooks installed by Entire on npm install.\n", m.Name)
    72→			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "  To make Entire hooks permanent, add these lines to your %s hook files:\n", m.Name)
    73→			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "\n")
    74→
    75→			// Use the config path as the hook directory prefix for hook files.
    76→			// For Husky, this is typically ".husky/" where hook scripts are stored.
    77→			hookDir := m.ConfigPath
    78→
    79→			for _, spec := range specs {
    80→				cmdLine := extractCommandLine(spec.content)
    81→				if cmdLine == "" {
    82→					continue
    83→				}
    84→				fmt.Fprintf(&b, "    %s%s:\n", hookDir, spec.name)
    85→				fmt.Fprintf(&b, "      %s\n", cmdLine)
    86→				fmt.Fprintf(&b, "\n")
    87→			}
    88→		} else {
    89→			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "Note: %s detected (%s)\n", m.Name, m.ConfigPath)
    90→			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "\n")
    91→			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "  If %s reinstalls hooks, run 'entire enable' to restore Entire's hooks.\n", m.Name)
    92→			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "\n")
    93→		}
    94→	}
    95→
    96→	return b.String()
    97→}
    98→
    99→// extractCommandLine returns the first non-shebang, non-comment, non-empty line
   100→// from a hook script. This is the actual command invocation line.
   101→func extractCommandLine(hookContent string) string {
   102→	for _, line := range strings.Split(hookContent, "\n") {
   103→		trimmed := strings.TrimSpace(line)
   104→		if trimmed == "" || strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, "#") {
   105→			continue
   106→		}
   107→		return trimmed
   108→	}
   109→	return ""
   110→}
   111→
   112→// CheckAndWarnHookManagers detects external hook managers and writes a warning
   113→// to w if any are found.
   114→func CheckAndWarnHookManagers(w io.Writer) {
   115→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
   116→	if err != nil {
   117→		return
   118→	}
   119→
   120→	managers := detectHookManagers(repoRoot)
   121→	if len(managers) == 0 {
   122→		return
   123→	}
   124→
   125→	warning := hookManagerWarning(managers, hookCmdPrefix())
   126→	if warning != "" {
   127→		fmt.Fprintln(w)
   128→		fmt.Fprint(w, warning)
   129→	}
   130→}
   131→

     1→package strategy
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"encoding/json"
     6→	"errors"
     7→	"fmt"
     8→	"os"
     9→	"os/exec"
    10→	"path/filepath"
    11→	"sort"
    12→	"strings"
    13→	"sync"
    14→	"time"
    15→
    16→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
    17→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint"
    18→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/id"
    19→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    20→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/trailers"

    22→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5"
    23→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing"
    24→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing/filemode"
    25→	"github.com/go-git/go-git/v5/plumbing/object"
    26→)
    27→
    28→// Common branch name constants for default branch detection.
    29→const (
    30→	branchMain   = "main"
    31→	branchMaster = "master"
    32→)
    33→
    34→// errStop is a sentinel error used to break out of git log iteration.
    35→// Shared across strategies that iterate through git commits.
    36→// NOTE: A similar sentinel exists in checkpoint/temporary.go - this is intentional.
    37→// Each package needs its own package-scoped sentinel for git log iteration patterns.
    38→var errStop = errors.New("stop iteration")
    39→
    40→// IsEmptyRepository returns true if the repository has no commits yet.
    41→// After git-init, HEAD points to an unborn branch (e.g., refs/heads/main)
    42→// whose target does not yet exist. repo.Head() returns ErrReferenceNotFound
    43→// in this case.
    44→func IsEmptyRepository(repo *git.Repository) bool {
    45→	_, err := repo.Head()
    46→	return errors.Is(err, plumbing.ErrReferenceNotFound)
    47→}
    48→
    49→// IsAncestorOf checks if commit is an ancestor of (or equal to) target.
    50→// Returns true if target can reach commit by following parent links.
    51→// Limits search to 1000 commits to avoid excessive traversal.
    52→func IsAncestorOf(repo *git.Repository, commit, target plumbing.Hash) bool {
    53→	if commit == target {
    54→		return true
    55→	}
    56→
    57→	iter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: target})
    58→	if err != nil {
    59→		return false
    60→	}
    61→	defer iter.Close()
    62→
    63→	found := false
    64→	count := 0
    65→	_ = iter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error { //nolint:errcheck // Best-effort search, errors are non-fatal
    66→		count++
    67→		if count > 1000 {
    68→			return errStop
    69→		}
    70→		if c.Hash == commit {
    71→			found = true
    72→			return errStop
    73→		}
    74→		return nil
    75→	})
    76→
    77→	return found
    78→}
    79→
    80→// ListCheckpoints returns all checkpoints from the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
    81→// Scans sharded paths: <id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/ directories containing metadata.json.
    82→// Used by both manual-commit and auto-commit strategies.
    83→func ListCheckpoints() ([]CheckpointInfo, error) {
    84→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
    85→	if err != nil {
    86→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open git repository: %w", err)
    87→	}
    88→
    89→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
    90→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
    91→	if err != nil {
    92→		//nolint:nilerr // No sessions branch yet is expected, return empty list
    93→		return []CheckpointInfo{}, nil
    94→	}
    95→
    96→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
    97→	if err != nil {
    98→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit object: %w", err)
    99→	}
   100→
   101→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   102→	if err != nil {
   103→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit tree: %w", err)
   104→	}
   105→
   106→	var checkpoints []CheckpointInfo
   107→
   108→	// Scan sharded structure: <2-char-prefix>/<remaining-id>/metadata.json
   109→	// The tree has 2-character directories (hex buckets)
   110→	for _, bucketEntry := range tree.Entries {
   111→		if bucketEntry.Mode != filemode.Dir {
   112→			continue
   113→		}
   114→		// Bucket should be 2 hex chars
   115→		if len(bucketEntry.Name) != 2 {
   116→			continue
   117→		}
   118→
   119→		bucketTree, treeErr := repo.TreeObject(bucketEntry.Hash)
   120→		if treeErr != nil {
   121→			continue
   122→		}
   123→
   124→		// Each entry in the bucket is the remaining part of the checkpoint ID
   125→		for _, checkpointEntry := range bucketTree.Entries {
   126→			if checkpointEntry.Mode != filemode.Dir {
   127→				continue
   128→			}
   129→
   130→			checkpointTree, cpTreeErr := repo.TreeObject(checkpointEntry.Hash)
   131→			if cpTreeErr != nil {
   132→				continue
   133→			}
   134→
   135→			// Reconstruct checkpoint ID: <bucket><remaining>
   136→			checkpointIDStr := bucketEntry.Name + checkpointEntry.Name
   137→			checkpointID, cpErr := id.NewCheckpointID(checkpointIDStr)
   138→			if cpErr != nil {
   139→				// Skip invalid checkpoint IDs
   140→				continue
   141→			}
   142→
   143→			info := CheckpointInfo{
   144→				CheckpointID: checkpointID,
   145→			}
   146→
   147→			// Get details from metadata file (CheckpointSummary format)
   148→			if metadataFile, fileErr := checkpointTree.File(paths.MetadataFileName); fileErr == nil {
   149→				if content, contentErr := metadataFile.Contents(); contentErr == nil {
   150→					var summary checkpoint.CheckpointSummary
   151→					if json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &summary) == nil && len(summary.Sessions) > 0 {
   152→						info.CheckpointsCount = summary.CheckpointsCount
   153→						info.FilesTouched = summary.FilesTouched
   154→						info.SessionCount = len(summary.Sessions)
   155→
   156→						// Read session-level metadata for Agent, SessionID, CreatedAt, SessionIDs
   157→						for i, sessionPaths := range summary.Sessions {
   158→							if sessionPaths.Metadata != "" {
   159→								// SessionFilePaths now contains absolute paths with leading "/"
   160→								// Strip the leading "/" for tree.File() which expects paths without leading slash
   161→								sessionMetadataPath := strings.TrimPrefix(sessionPaths.Metadata, "/")
   162→								if sessionFile, sErr := tree.File(sessionMetadataPath); sErr == nil {
   163→									if sessionContent, scErr := sessionFile.Contents(); scErr == nil {
   164→										var sessionMetadata checkpoint.CommittedMetadata
   165→										if json.Unmarshal([]byte(sessionContent), &sessionMetadata) == nil {
   166→											info.SessionIDs = append(info.SessionIDs, sessionMetadata.SessionID)
   167→											// Use first session's metadata for Agent, SessionID, CreatedAt
   168→											if i == 0 {
   169→												info.Agent = sessionMetadata.Agent
   170→												info.SessionID = sessionMetadata.SessionID
   171→												info.CreatedAt = sessionMetadata.CreatedAt
   172→												info.IsTask = sessionMetadata.IsTask
   173→												info.ToolUseID = sessionMetadata.ToolUseID
   174→											}
   175→										}
   176→									}
   177→								}
   178→							}
   179→						}
   180→					}
   181→				}
   182→			}
   183→
   184→			checkpoints = append(checkpoints, info)
   185→		}
   186→	}
   187→
   188→	// Sort by time (most recent first)
   189→	sort.Slice(checkpoints, func(i, j int) bool {
   190→		return checkpoints[i].CreatedAt.After(checkpoints[j].CreatedAt)
   191→	})
   192→
   193→	return checkpoints, nil
   194→}
   195→
   196→const (
   197→	entireGitignore    = ".entire/.gitignore"
   198→	entireDir          = ".entire"
   199→	gitDir             = ".git"
   200→	shadowBranchPrefix = "entire/"
   201→
   202→	// DefaultAgentType is the generic fallback agent type name
   203→	DefaultAgentType = agent.AgentTypeUnknown
   204→)
   205→
   206→// isProtectedPath returns true if relPath is inside a directory that should
   207→// never be modified or deleted during rewind or other destructive operations.
   208→// Protected directories include git internals, entire metadata, and all
   209→// registered agent config directories.
   210→func isProtectedPath(relPath string) bool {
   211→	for _, dir := range protectedDirs() {
   212→		if relPath == dir || strings.HasPrefix(relPath, dir+string(filepath.Separator)) {
   213→			return true
   214→		}
   215→	}
   216→	return false
   217→}
   218→
   219→// protectedDirs returns the list of directories to protect. This combines
   220→// static infrastructure dirs with agent-reported dirs from the registry.
   221→// The result is cached via sync.Once since it's called per-file when filtering untracked files.
   222→//
   223→// NOTE: The cache is never invalidated. In production this is fine (the agent registry
   224→// is populated at init time and never changes). However, tests that mutate the agent
   225→// registry after the first call to protectedDirs/isProtectedPath will see stale results.
   226→// If you need to test isProtectedPath with a custom registry, either:
   227→//   - run those tests in a separate process, or
   228→//   - call resetProtectedDirsForTest() to clear the cache.
   229→func protectedDirs() []string {
   230→	protectedDirsOnce.Do(func() {
   231→		protectedDirsCache = append([]string{gitDir, entireDir}, agent.AllProtectedDirs()...)
   232→	})
   233→	return protectedDirsCache
   234→}
   235→
   236→var (
   237→	protectedDirsOnce  sync.Once
   238→	protectedDirsCache []string
   239→)
   240→
   241→// isSpecificAgentType returns true if the agent type is a known, specific value
   242→// (not empty and not the generic "Agent" fallback).
   243→func isSpecificAgentType(t agent.AgentType) bool {
   244→	return t != "" && t != DefaultAgentType
   245→}
   246→
   247→// resolveAgentType picks the best agent type from the context and existing state.
   248→// Priority: existing state (if specific) > context value > default fallback.
   249→func resolveAgentType(ctxAgentType agent.AgentType, state *SessionState) agent.AgentType {
   250→	if state != nil && isSpecificAgentType(state.AgentType) {
   251→		return state.AgentType
   252→	}
   253→	if ctxAgentType != "" {
   254→		return ctxAgentType
   255→	}
   256→	return DefaultAgentType
   257→}
   258→
   259→// ensureMetadataBranch creates the orphan entire/checkpoints/v1 branch if it doesn't exist.
   260→// This branch has no parent and starts with an empty tree.
   261→func EnsureMetadataBranch(repo *git.Repository) error {
   262→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
   263→
   264→	// Check if branch already exists
   265→	_, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   266→	if err == nil {
   267→		// Branch already exists
   268→		return nil
   269→	}
   270→
   271→	// Create empty tree (no files)
   272→	emptyTree := &object.Tree{Entries: []object.TreeEntry{}}
   273→	obj := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   274→	if err := emptyTree.Encode(obj); err != nil {
   275→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to encode empty tree: %w", err)
   276→	}
   277→	emptyTreeHash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(obj)
   278→	if err != nil {
   279→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to store empty tree: %w", err)
   280→	}
   281→
   282→	// Create orphan commit (no parent)
   283→	now := time.Now()
   284→	authorName, authorEmail := GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo)
   285→	sig := object.Signature{
   286→		Name:  authorName,
   287→		Email: authorEmail,
   288→		When:  now,
   289→	}
   290→
   291→	commit := &object.Commit{
   292→		TreeHash:  emptyTreeHash,
   293→		Author:    sig,
   294→		Committer: sig,
   295→		Message:   "Initialize metadata branch\n\nThis branch stores session metadata for the auto-commit strategy.\n",
   296→	}
   297→	// Note: No ParentHashes - this is an orphan commit
   298→
   299→	commitObj := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   300→	if err := commit.Encode(commitObj); err != nil {
   301→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to encode orphan commit: %w", err)
   302→	}
   303→	commitHash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(commitObj)
   304→	if err != nil {
   305→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to store orphan commit: %w", err)
   306→	}
   307→
   308→	// Create branch reference
   309→	ref := plumbing.NewHashReference(refName, commitHash)
   310→	if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(ref); err != nil {
   311→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create metadata branch: %w", err)
   312→	}
   313→
   314→	fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "✓ Created orphan branch '%s' for session metadata\n", paths.MetadataBranchName)
   315→	return nil
   316→}
   317→
   318→// readCheckpointMetadata reads metadata.json from a checkpoint path on entire/checkpoints/v1.
   319→// With the new format, root metadata.json is a CheckpointSummary with Agents array.
   320→// This function reads the summary and extracts relevant fields into CheckpointInfo,
   321→// also reading session-level metadata for IsTask/ToolUseID fields.
   322→func ReadCheckpointMetadata(tree *object.Tree, checkpointPath string) (*CheckpointInfo, error) {
   323→	metadataPath := checkpointPath + "/metadata.json"
   324→	file, err := tree.File(metadataPath)
   325→	if err != nil {
   326→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to find metadata at %s: %w", metadataPath, err)
   327→	}
   328→
   329→	content, err := file.Contents()
   330→	if err != nil {
   331→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read metadata: %w", err)
   332→	}
   333→
   334→	// Try to parse as CheckpointSummary first (new format)
   335→	var summary checkpoint.CheckpointSummary
   336→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &summary); err == nil {
   337→		// If we have sessions array, this is the new format
   338→		if len(summary.Sessions) > 0 {
   339→			info := &CheckpointInfo{
   340→				CheckpointID:     summary.CheckpointID,
   341→				CheckpointsCount: summary.CheckpointsCount,
   342→				FilesTouched:     summary.FilesTouched,
   343→				SessionCount:     len(summary.Sessions),
   344→			}
   345→
   346→			// Read all sessions' metadata to populate SessionIDs and get other fields from first session
   347→			var sessionIDs []string
   348→			for i, sessionPaths := range summary.Sessions {
   349→				if sessionPaths.Metadata != "" {
   350→					// SessionFilePaths now contains absolute paths with leading "/"
   351→					// Strip the leading "/" for tree.File() which expects paths without leading slash
   352→					sessionMetadataPath := strings.TrimPrefix(sessionPaths.Metadata, "/")
   353→					if sessionFile, err := tree.File(sessionMetadataPath); err == nil {
   354→						if sessionContent, err := sessionFile.Contents(); err == nil {
   355→							var sessionMetadata checkpoint.CommittedMetadata
   356→							if json.Unmarshal([]byte(sessionContent), &sessionMetadata) == nil {
   357→								sessionIDs = append(sessionIDs, sessionMetadata.SessionID)
   358→								// Use first session for Agent, SessionID, CreatedAt, IsTask, ToolUseID
   359→								if i == 0 {
   360→									info.Agent = sessionMetadata.Agent
   361→									info.SessionID = sessionMetadata.SessionID
   362→									info.CreatedAt = sessionMetadata.CreatedAt
   363→									info.IsTask = sessionMetadata.IsTask
   364→									info.ToolUseID = sessionMetadata.ToolUseID
   365→								}
   366→							}
   367→						}
   368→					}
   369→				}
   370→			}
   371→			info.SessionIDs = sessionIDs
   372→
   373→			return info, nil
   374→		}
   375→	}
   376→
   377→	// Fall back to parsing as CheckpointInfo (old format or direct info)
   378→	var metadata CheckpointInfo
   379→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &metadata); err != nil {
   380→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse metadata: %w", err)
   381→	}
   382→
   383→	return &metadata, nil
   384→}
   385→
   386→// GetMetadataBranchTree returns the tree object for the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
   387→func GetMetadataBranchTree(repo *git.Repository) (*object.Tree, error) {
   388→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
   389→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   390→	if err != nil {
   391→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get metadata branch reference: %w", err)
   392→	}
   393→
   394→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   395→	if err != nil {
   396→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get metadata branch commit: %w", err)
   397→	}
   398→
   399→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   400→	if err != nil {
   401→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get metadata branch tree: %w", err)
   402→	}
   403→	return tree, nil
   404→}
   405→
   406→// ExtractFirstPrompt extracts and truncates the first meaningful prompt from prompt content.
   407→// Prompts are separated by "\n\n---\n\n". Skips empty prompts and separator-only content.
   408→// Returns empty string if no valid prompt is found.
   409→func ExtractFirstPrompt(content string) string {
   410→	if content == "" {
   411→		return ""
   412→	}
   413→
   414→	// Prompts are separated by "\n\n---\n\n"
   415→	// Find the first non-empty prompt
   416→	prompts := strings.Split(content, "\n\n---\n\n")
   417→	var firstPrompt string
   418→	for _, p := range prompts {
   419→		cleaned := strings.TrimSpace(p)
   420→		// Skip empty prompts or prompts that are just dashes/separators
   421→		if cleaned == "" || isOnlySeparators(cleaned) {
   422→			continue
   423→		}
   424→		firstPrompt = cleaned
   425→		break
   426→	}
   427→
   428→	if firstPrompt == "" {
   429→		return ""
   430→	}
   431→
   432→	return TruncateDescription(firstPrompt, MaxDescriptionLength)
   433→}
   434→
   435→// ReadSessionPromptFromTree reads the first meaningful prompt from a checkpoint's prompt.txt file in a git tree.
   436→// Returns an empty string if the prompt cannot be read.
   437→func ReadSessionPromptFromTree(tree *object.Tree, checkpointPath string) string {
   438→	promptPath := checkpointPath + "/" + paths.PromptFileName
   439→	file, err := tree.File(promptPath)
   440→	if err != nil {
   441→		return ""
   442→	}
   443→
   444→	content, err := file.Contents()
   445→	if err != nil {
   446→		return ""
   447→	}
   448→
   449→	return ExtractFirstPrompt(content)
   450→}
   451→
   452→// ReadAgentTypeFromTree reads the agent type from a checkpoint's metadata.json file in a git tree.
   453→// If metadata.json doesn't exist (shadow branches), it falls back to detecting the agent
   454→// from the presence of agent-specific config files (.gemini/settings.json or .claude/).
   455→// Returns agent.AgentTypeUnknown if the agent type cannot be determined.
   456→func ReadAgentTypeFromTree(tree *object.Tree, checkpointPath string) agent.AgentType {
   457→	// First, try to read from metadata.json (present in condensed/committed checkpoints)
   458→	metadataPath := checkpointPath + "/" + paths.MetadataFileName
   459→	if file, err := tree.File(metadataPath); err == nil {
   460→		if content, err := file.Contents(); err == nil {
   461→			var metadata checkpoint.CommittedMetadata
   462→			if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &metadata); err == nil && metadata.Agent != "" {
   463→				return metadata.Agent
   464→			}
   465→		}
   466→	}
   467→
   468→	// Fall back to detecting agent from config files (shadow branches don't have metadata.json).
   469→	// Order: Gemini (most specific check), Claude (established default), OpenCode (newest/preview).
   470→	if _, err := tree.File(".gemini/settings.json"); err == nil {
   471→		return agent.AgentTypeGemini
   472→	}
   473→	if _, err := tree.Tree(".claude"); err == nil {
   474→		return agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode
   475→	}
   476→	// OpenCode: .opencode directory or opencode.json config
   477→	if _, err := tree.Tree(".opencode"); err == nil {
   478→		return agent.AgentTypeOpenCode
   479→	}
   480→	if _, err := tree.File("opencode.json"); err == nil {
   481→		return agent.AgentTypeOpenCode
   482→	}
   483→
   484→	return agent.AgentTypeUnknown
   485→}
   486→
   487→// isOnlySeparators checks if a string contains only dashes, spaces, and newlines.
   488→func isOnlySeparators(s string) bool {
   489→	for _, r := range s {
   490→		if r != '-' && r != ' ' && r != '\n' && r != '\r' && r != '\t' {
   491→			return false
   492→		}
   493→	}
   494→	return true
   495→}
   496→
   497→// ReadAllSessionPromptsFromTree reads the first prompt for all sessions in a multi-session checkpoint.
   498→// Returns a slice of prompts parallel to sessionIDs (oldest to newest).
   499→// For single-session checkpoints, returns a slice with just the root prompt.
   500→func ReadAllSessionPromptsFromTree(tree *object.Tree, checkpointPath string, sessionCount int, sessionIDs []string) []string {
   501→	if sessionCount <= 1 || len(sessionIDs) <= 1 {
   502→		// Single session - just return the root prompt
   503→		prompt := ReadSessionPromptFromTree(tree, checkpointPath)
   504→		if prompt != "" {
   505→			return []string{prompt}
   506→		}
   507→		return nil
   508→	}
   509→
   510→	// Multi-session: read prompts from archived folders (0/, 1/, etc.) and root
   511→	prompts := make([]string, len(sessionIDs))
   512→
   513→	// Read archived session prompts (folders 0, 1, ... N-2)
   514→	for i := range sessionCount - 1 {
   515→		archivedPath := fmt.Sprintf("%s/%d", checkpointPath, i)
   516→		prompts[i] = ReadSessionPromptFromTree(tree, archivedPath)
   517→	}
   518→
   519→	// Read the most recent session prompt (at root level)
   520→	prompts[len(prompts)-1] = ReadSessionPromptFromTree(tree, checkpointPath)
   521→
   522→	return prompts
   523→}
