```
Now I have a thorough understanding of the full execution path. Let me compile a comprehensive report.

---

## Full Execution Path: Claude Code Hooks (Session Start and Stop)

### 1. Hook Installation (`InstallHooks`)

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/hooks.go` (lines 46-225)

The `ClaudeCodeAgent.InstallHooks()` method writes entries into `.claude/settings.json` at the repo root. In production mode, the following hook commands are installed:

| Claude Code Event     | Command Installed                                          | Matcher  |
|-----------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|----------|
| `SessionStart`        | `entire hooks claude-code session-start`                  | (none)   |
| `SessionEnd`          | `entire hooks claude-code session-end`                    | (none)   |
| `Stop`                | `entire hooks claude-code stop`                           | (none)   |
| `UserPromptSubmit`    | `entire hooks claude-code user-prompt-submit`             | (none)   |
| `PreToolUse`          | `entire hooks claude-code pre-task`                       | `Task`   |
| `PostToolUse`         | `entire hooks claude-code post-task`                      | `Task`   |
| `PostToolUse`         | `entire hooks claude-code post-todo`                      | `TodoWrite` |

It also installs a `permissions.deny` rule: `Read(./.entire/metadata/**)` to block Claude from reading session metadata.

In local-dev mode, the commands use `go run ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/cmd/entire/main.go hooks claude-code ...` instead.

The JSON structure stored is an array of `ClaudeHookMatcher` objects, each with an optional `matcher` string (tool name for PreToolUse/PostToolUse) and an array of `hooks` entries (type: "command", command: the string above).

---

### 2. Hook Command Registration

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/hooks_cmd.go` (lines 13-35)

`newHooksCmd()` creates the `entire hooks` command, then dynamically adds subcommands for each registered agent (claude-code, gemini, opencode). For each agent, it calls `newAgentHooksCmd()`.

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry.go` (lines 47-71)

`newAgentHooksCmd()` creates the `entire hooks claude-code` subcommand with:
- `PersistentPreRunE`: calls `initHookLogging()` which sets up the log-level getter and initializes file-based logging
- `PersistentPostRunE`: calls `logging.Close()` to flush logs
- For each hook name returned by `agent.HookNames()` (session-start, session-end, stop, user-prompt-submit, pre-task, post-task, post-todo), adds a subcommand via `newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging()`.

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry.go` (lines 91-160)

`newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging()` creates the actual leaf command (e.g., `entire hooks claude-code stop`). Its `RunE` function is the entry point for every hook invocation:

1. **Skip if not in a git repo**: `paths.RepoRoot()` check -- returns nil (no error) if not in git repo
2. **Skip if Entire not enabled**: `IsEnabled()` loads `.entire/settings.json` and checks `Enabled` field
3. **Timing**: Captures start time
4. **Logging context**: Creates context with agent name and component "hooks"
5. **Get strategy name**: `GetStrategy()` loads settings, resolves strategy (defaults to manual-commit)
6. **Set current hook agent**: Sets `currentHookAgentName` package variable so downstream code can identify the agent
7. **Parse hook event**: Calls `ag.ParseHookEvent(hookName, cmd.InOrStdin())` to parse JSON from stdin into a normalized `agent.Event`
8. **Dispatch**:
   - If event is non-nil: calls `DispatchLifecycleEvent(ag, event)`
   - Special case: if Claude Code + PostTodo: calls `handleClaudeCodePostTodo()` directly
   - Otherwise: no-op (pass-through hook)
9. **Log duration**

---

### 3. `UserPromptSubmit` Hook (Turn Start / Session Start)

**Hook script runs**: `entire hooks claude-code user-prompt-submit`

**Claude Code sends on stdin** (parsed by `parseTurnStart` in lifecycle.go):
```json
{
  "session_id": "...",
  "transcript_path": "/path/to/.jsonl",
  "prompt": "user's prompt text"
}
```

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/lifecycle.go` (lines 155-167)

`ParseHookEvent("user-prompt-submit", stdin)` calls `parseTurnStart()` which:
- Reads stdin JSON into `userPromptSubmitRaw` struct
- Returns `agent.Event{Type: agent.TurnStart, SessionID, SessionRef (transcript path), Prompt, Timestamp}`

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go` (lines 112-148)

`handleLifecycleTurnStart(ag, event)` does the following:

#### Step 3a: Validate
- Logs "turn-start" at INFO level
- Validates `event.SessionID` is non-empty and passes `validation.ValidateSessionID()`

#### Step 3b: Capture Pre-Prompt State
**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/state.go` (lines 92-146)

`CapturePrePromptState(ag, sessionID, sessionRef)`:
1. Creates `.entire/tmp/` directory (via `paths.AbsPath(paths.EntireTmpDir)`)
2. **Git status scan**: `getUntrackedFilesForState()` opens the git repo with go-git, calls `worktree.Status()`, collects all untracked files (excluding `.entire/` directory)
3. **Transcript position**: If agent implements `TranscriptAnalyzer` (Claude Code does), calls `analyzer.GetTranscriptPosition(sessionRef)` which counts lines in the JSONL transcript file
4. **Writes state file**: Saves `PrePromptState{SessionID, Timestamp, UntrackedFiles, TranscriptOffset}` to `.entire/tmp/pre-prompt-<sessionID>.json`

#### Step 3c: Strategy EnsureSetup
**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit.go` (lines 98-120)

`strat.EnsureSetup()` (ManualCommitStrategy):
1. **Gitignore**: `EnsureEntireGitignore()` -- ensures `.entire/` is in `.gitignore`
2. **Metadata branch**: Opens git repo, calls `EnsureMetadataBranch(repo)` to ensure the orphan `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch exists
3. **Git hooks**: Checks if git hooks are installed (`IsGitHookInstalled()`), if not calls `InstallGitHook(true)` which installs prepare-commit-msg, commit-msg, post-commit, and pre-push hooks that delegate to `entire hooks git ...`

#### Step 3d: Initialize Session (SessionInitializer)
**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` (lines 1397-1486)

`initializer.InitializeSession(sessionID, agentType, sessionRef, prompt)` (ManualCommitStrategy):

**Case 1: Existing session (state found with BaseCommit set)**
1. Loads session state from `.git/entire-sessions/<sessionID>.json`
2. Fires `session.EventTurnStart` transition on the state machine (IDLE -> ACTIVE, or ACTIVE -> ACTIVE for Ctrl-C recovery)
3. Generates a new `TurnID` (random 12-hex ID)
4. Backfills `AgentType` and `FirstPrompt` if empty
5. Updates `TranscriptPath` if changed
6. Clears `LastCheckpointID` and `TurnCheckpointIDs` (fresh turn)
7. **Calculates prompt attribution**: `calculatePromptAttributionAtStart()` -- reads the shadow branch tip tree (last checkpoint), base commit tree, and current worktree status to compute line-level attribution (user vs agent changes). Stores in `state.PendingPromptAttribution`
8. **Shadow branch migration**: `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()` -- if HEAD moved (pull/rebase), renames shadow branch from `entire/<old-hash>-<worktreeHash>` to `entire/<new-hash>-<worktreeHash>`
9. Saves updated session state

**Case 2: New session (no state or empty BaseCommit)**
1. Calls private `initializeSession()`:

   **File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_session.go` (lines 195-249)
   
   - Gets HEAD hash from git
   - Gets worktree path and worktree ID (for multi-worktree support)
   - Collects untracked files (for rewind preservation)
   - Generates a `TurnID`
   - Creates `SessionState` with: SessionID, CLIVersion, BaseCommit (HEAD hash), WorktreePath, WorktreeID, StartedAt, TurnID, StepCount=0, UntrackedFilesAtStart, AgentType, TranscriptPath, FirstPrompt
   - Saves to `.git/entire-sessions/<sessionID>.json`
2. Fires `session.EventTurnStart` transition (IDLE -> ACTIVE)
3. Calculates prompt attribution
4. Saves state

---

### 4. `SessionStart` Hook

**Hook script runs**: `entire hooks claude-code session-start`

**Claude Code sends on stdin** (parsed by `parseSessionStart` in lifecycle.go):
```json
{
  "session_id": "...",
  "transcript_path": "/path/to/.jsonl"
}
```

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/lifecycle.go` (lines 142-153)

`ParseHookEvent("session-start", stdin)` calls `parseSessionStart()` which returns `agent.Event{Type: agent.SessionStart, SessionID, SessionRef, Timestamp}`.

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go` (lines 61-108)

`handleLifecycleSessionStart(ag, event)`:

1. **Validates** session_id is present and valid
2. **Builds banner message**: `"\n\nPowered by Entire:\n  This conversation will be linked to your next commit."`
3. **Concurrent session check**: If strategy implements `ConcurrentSessionChecker`, calls `CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints(sessionID)` to count other active sessions on same base commit. If >0, appends count to the message.
4. **Outputs JSON response to stdout**: `outputHookResponse(message)` which writes `{"systemMessage": "..."}` -- Claude Code displays this as a system message to the user
5. **State machine transition**: Loads session state for this session ID. If state exists, fires `session.EventSessionStart` transition (which may clear EndedAt if session was previously ended). Saves the state.

Note: SessionStart fires before UserPromptSubmit. SessionStart shows the banner; UserPromptSubmit does the heavy initialization (state capture, strategy setup, session init).

---

### 5. `Stop` Hook (Turn End)

**Hook script runs**: `entire hooks claude-code stop`

**Claude Code sends on stdin**:
```json
{
  "session_id": "...",
  "transcript_path": "/path/to/.jsonl"
}
```

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/lifecycle.go` (lines 169-179)

`ParseHookEvent("stop", stdin)` calls `parseTurnEnd()` which returns `agent.Event{Type: agent.TurnEnd, SessionID, SessionRef, Timestamp}`.

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go` (lines 154-424)

`handleLifecycleTurnEnd(ag, event)` -- this is the most complex handler. Full call chain:

#### Step 5a: Validation
- Validates transcript file exists
- **Empty repo check**: Opens repository, checks `IsEmptyRepository()` -- if no commits yet, prints "skipping checkpoint" and returns

#### Step 5b: Metadata Directory Creation
- Creates `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/` directory

#### Step 5c: Transcript Preparation
- If agent implements `TranscriptPreparer` (Claude Code does), calls `PrepareTranscript(transcriptRef)`:

  **File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/lifecycle.go` (lines 126-129, 237-312)
  
  `waitForTranscriptFlush(sessionRef, time.Now())`: Polls the transcript file (up to 3 seconds, every 50ms) looking for a `hook_progress` sentinel entry containing `"hooks claude-code stop"` with a recent timestamp. This ensures Claude Code's async transcript writer has flushed all pending writes before we read it.

#### Step 5d: Copy Transcript
- `ag.ReadTranscript(transcriptRef)` reads the JSONL file
- Writes to `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/full.jsonl`

#### Step 5e: Copy Export JSON (OpenCode only)
- If `<transcriptRef>.export.json` exists, copies it too

#### Step 5f: Load Pre-Prompt State
- `LoadPrePromptState(sessionID)` reads `.entire/tmp/pre-prompt-<sessionID>.json`
- Uses the `TranscriptOffset` from that state

#### Step 5g: Extract Metadata via Agent Interfaces
Using `TranscriptAnalyzer` and `SubagentAwareExtractor`:
1. **Prompts**: `analyzer.ExtractPrompts(transcriptRef, transcriptOffset)` -- parses JSONL from the offset, extracts user messages
2. **Summary**: `analyzer.ExtractSummary(transcriptRef)` -- finds last assistant text block
3. **Modified files**: `subagentExtractor.ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptRef, transcriptOffset, subagentsDir)` -- extracts files from Write/Edit tool uses in both main agent and subagent transcripts
4. **Transcript position**: Gets current line count

#### Step 5h: Write Metadata Files
- Writes `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/prompt.txt` (all prompts joined with `---`)
- Writes `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/summary.txt`
- Generates commit message from last prompt

#### Step 5i: Detect File Changes
- Gets repo root via `paths.RepoRoot()`
- **Git status via go-git**: `DetectFileChanges(preUntrackedFiles)` opens repo, gets worktree status, categorizes files into Modified, New (untracked minus pre-existing), Deleted
- **Filters paths**: `FilterAndNormalizePaths()` converts absolute to relative, excludes `.entire/` paths
- **Filters committed files**: `filterToUncommittedFiles()` compares working tree content with HEAD to exclude files already committed mid-turn
- If zero total changes: transitions session phase, cleans up pre-prompt state, returns (no checkpoint)

#### Step 5j: Create Context File
- Writes `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/context.md` with session info, prompts, and summary

#### Step 5k: Get Git Author
- `GetGitAuthor()` reads from git config

#### Step 5l: Calculate Token Usage
- If agent implements `SubagentAwareExtractor`: `CalculateTotalTokenUsage(transcriptRef, transcriptLinesAtStart, subagentsDir)` -- sums up input/output/cache tokens from all API responses in the transcript (including subagent transcripts)

#### Step 5m: Save Step (Strategy)
**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` (lines 24-120+)

`strat.SaveStep(ctx)` (ManualCommitStrategy):
1. Opens git repository
2. Loads session state; initializes if needed
3. Checks for HEAD migration (`migrateAndPersistIfNeeded`)
4. Gets checkpoint store
5. Uses pending prompt attribution from InitializeSession
6. Calls `store.WriteTemporary()` which:
   - Builds an in-memory git tree containing all modified/new files + metadata directory
   - Creates a commit on the shadow branch `entire/<HEAD-hash[:7]>-<worktreeHash[:6]>` with trailers (Entire-Session, Entire-Metadata, Entire-Strategy)
   - Deduplication: if tree hash matches previous commit, skips
7. Updates session state: increments StepCount, updates transcript position
8. Saves session state to `.git/entire-sessions/<sessionID>.json`

#### Step 5n: Session Phase Transition and Cleanup
- **Auto-commit transcript position update**: If strategy is auto-commit, updates `CheckpointTranscriptStart` in session state
- `transitionSessionTurnEnd(sessionID)`:
  1. Loads session state
  2. Fires `session.EventTurnEnd` transition: ACTIVE -> IDLE with `ActionUpdateLastInteraction`
  3. If strategy implements `TurnEndHandler`, calls `HandleTurnEnd(state)` which finalizes all turn checkpoints with the full transcript (replaces provisional transcript data written by PostCommit during mid-turn commits)
  4. Saves session state
- `CleanupPrePromptState(sessionID)`: Deletes `.entire/tmp/pre-prompt-<sessionID>.json`

---

### 6. `SessionEnd` Hook

**Hook script runs**: `entire hooks claude-code session-end`

**File:** `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go` (lines 460-475)

`handleLifecycleSessionEnd(ag, event)`:
1. Logs "session-end"
2. If no session ID, returns nil
3. Calls `markSessionEnded(sessionID)`:
   - Loads session state
   - Fires `session.EventSessionStop` transition: any -> ENDED with `ActionUpdateLastInteraction`
   - Sets `state.EndedAt` to current time
   - Saves session state

---

### Summary of the Full Call Chain

**Session Start** (two hooks fire in sequence):

```
Claude Code SessionStart hook
  -> stdin JSON -> parseSessionStart() -> Event{SessionStart}
  -> handleLifecycleSessionStart()
     -> Validate session ID
     -> Check concurrent sessions (strategy.ConcurrentSessionChecker)
     -> Output JSON banner to stdout (shown to user)
     -> Load session state, fire EventSessionStart transition, save state

Claude Code UserPromptSubmit hook  
  -> stdin JSON -> parseTurnStart() -> Event{TurnStart}
  -> handleLifecycleTurnStart()
     -> Validate session ID
     -> CapturePrePromptState():
        -> git status (go-git) -> collect untracked files
        -> GetTranscriptPosition() -> count JSONL lines
        -> Write .entire/tmp/pre-prompt-<sid>.json
     -> GetStrategy().EnsureSetup():
        -> Ensure .gitignore
        -> Ensure entire/checkpoints/v1 orphan branch
        -> Ensure git hooks (prepare-commit-msg, commit-msg, post-commit, pre-push)
     -> strategy.InitializeSession():
        -> Load/create session state in .git/entire-sessions/<sid>.json
        -> Fire EventTurnStart transition (-> ACTIVE)
        -> Generate TurnID, backfill AgentType/FirstPrompt
        -> Calculate prompt attribution (worktree diff vs last checkpoint)
        -> Migrate shadow branch if HEAD changed
        -> Save session state
```

**Session Stop** (two hooks fire in sequence):

```
Claude Code Stop hook
  -> stdin JSON -> parseTurnEnd() -> Event{TurnEnd}
  -> handleLifecycleTurnEnd()
     -> Validate transcript exists
     -> Check for empty repository
     -> Create .entire/metadata/<sid>/ directory
     -> PrepareTranscript(): poll for flush sentinel (up to 3s)
     -> ReadTranscript(): read JSONL file
     -> Copy transcript to .entire/metadata/<sid>/full.jsonl
     -> Load pre-prompt state (.entire/tmp/pre-prompt-<sid>.json)
     -> Extract prompts, summary, modified files from transcript
     -> Write prompt.txt, summary.txt, context.md
     -> DetectFileChanges(): git status via go-git
     -> FilterAndNormalizePaths(), filterToUncommittedFiles()
     -> Calculate token usage from transcript
     -> strategy.SaveStep():
        -> WriteTemporary() to shadow branch entire/<hash>-<wtid>
        -> Update session state (StepCount++, transcript position)
     -> transitionSessionTurnEnd():
        -> Fire EventTurnEnd (ACTIVE -> IDLE)
        -> HandleTurnEnd(): finalize turn checkpoints with full transcript
        -> Save session state
     -> CleanupPrePromptState(): delete .entire/tmp/pre-prompt-<sid>.json

Claude Code SessionEnd hook
  -> stdin JSON -> parseSessionEnd() -> Event{SessionEnd}
  -> handleLifecycleSessionEnd()
     -> markSessionEnded():
        -> Load session state
        -> Fire EventSessionStop (any -> ENDED)
        -> Set EndedAt timestamp
        -> Save session state
```

> AGENT

This is very helpful. Here's what's happening on the critical path:

## The Problem

Every hook invocation spawns a full `entire` CLI process. On startup, Claude Code fires **two hooks sequentially**:

1. **`SessionStart`** — lighter (banner output, concurrent session check)
2. **`UserPromptSubmit`** — heavy:
   - `paths.RepoRoot()` → git subprocess
   - `IsEnabled()` → reads settings files
   - `GetStrategy()` → reads settings files again
   - `ParseHookEvent()` → reads stdin JSON
   - `CapturePrePromptState()` → opens repo with go-git, runs `worktree.Status()`, counts transcript lines, writes state file
   - `EnsureSetup()` → `EnsureEntireGitignore()`, `OpenRepository()` (another git subprocess), `EnsureMetadataBranch()`, `IsGitHookInstalled()` (another git subprocess + 4 file reads)
   - `InitializeSession()` → loads/saves session state, calculates prompt attribution (reads shadow branch tree + HEAD tree + worktree status)

On exit, two more hooks fire: **`Stop`** (very heavy — transcript polling, git status, SaveStep) and **`SessionEnd`** (light).

The user feels both the startup delay (before they can type) and the TTFB delay (before Claude responds after typing).

## What I'd Recommend

Let me create benchmarks for the actual hook commands — measuring the real wall-clock time of `entire hooks claude-code session-start` and `entire hooks claude-code user-prompt-submit` with realistic stdin input, the way Claude Code actually invokes them.

Want me to go ahead and build those hook benchmarks? We'd measure:
1. Each hook individually (session-start, user-prompt-submit, stop, session-end)
2. The combined startup pair (session-start + user-prompt-submit)
3. The combined exit pair (stop + session-end)

    98→// EnsureSetup ensures the strategy is properly set up.
    99→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) EnsureSetup() error {
   100→	if err := EnsureEntireGitignore(); err != nil {
   101→		return err

   165→// InstallGitHook installs generic git hooks that delegate to `entire hook` commands.
   166→// These hooks work with any strategy - the strategy is determined at runtime.
   167→// If silent is true, no output is printed (except backup notifications, which always print).
   168→// Returns the number of hooks that were installed (0 if all already up to date).
   169→func InstallGitHook(silent bool) (int, error) {
   170→	hooksDir, err := GetHooksDir()
   171→	if err != nil {
   172→		return 0, err
   173→	}
   174→
   175→	if err := os.MkdirAll(hooksDir, 0o755); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // Git hooks require executable permissions
   176→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to create hooks directory: %w", err)
   177→	}
   178→
   179→	specs := buildHookSpecs(hookCmdPrefix())
   180→	installedCount := 0
   181→
   182→	for _, spec := range specs {
   183→		hookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, spec.name)
   184→		backupPath := hookPath + backupSuffix
   185→		backupExists := fileExists(backupPath)
   186→
   187→		// Back up existing non-Entire hooks
   188→		existing, existingErr := os.ReadFile(hookPath) //nolint:gosec // path is controlled
   189→		if existingErr == nil && !strings.Contains(string(existing), entireHookMarker) {
   190→			if !backupExists {
   191→				if err := os.Rename(hookPath, backupPath); err != nil {
   192→					return installedCount, fmt.Errorf("failed to back up %s: %w", spec.name, err)
   193→				}
   194→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Backed up existing %s to %s%s\n", spec.name, spec.name, backupSuffix)
   195→			} else {
   196→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: replacing %s (backup %s%s already exists from a previous install)\n", spec.name, spec.name, backupSuffix)
   197→			}
   198→			backupExists = true
   199→		}
   200→
   201→		// Chain to backup if one exists
   202→		content := spec.content
   203→		if backupExists {
   204→			content = generateChainedContent(spec.content, spec.name)
   205→		}
   206→
   207→		written, err := writeHookFile(hookPath, content)
   208→		if err != nil {
   209→			return installedCount, fmt.Errorf("failed to install %s hook: %w", spec.name, err)
   210→		}
   211→		if written {
   212→			installedCount++
   213→		}
   214→	}
   215→
   216→	if !silent {
   217→		fmt.Println("✓ Installed git hooks (prepare-commit-msg, commit-msg, post-commit, pre-push)")
   218→		fmt.Println("  Hooks delegate to the current strategy at runtime")
   219→	}
   220→
   221→	return installedCount, nil
   222→}
```

   299→// hookCmdPrefix returns the command prefix for hook scripts and warning messages.
   300→// Returns "go run ./cmd/entire/main.go" when local_dev is enabled, "entire" otherwise.
   301→func hookCmdPrefix() string {
   302→	if isLocalDev() {

   310→func isLocalDev() bool {
   311→	s, err := settings.Load()
   312→	if err != nil {

   314→	}

     1→// Package settings provides configuration loading for Entire.
     2→// This package is separate from cli to allow strategy package to import it
     3→// without creating an import cycle (cli imports strategy).
     4→package settings
     5→
     6→import (
     7→	"bytes"
     8→	"encoding/json"
     9→	"fmt"
    10→	"os"
    11→	"path/filepath"
    12→
    13→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/jsonutil"
    14→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
    15→)
    16→
    17→// DefaultStrategyName is the default strategy when none is configured.
    18→// This is duplicated here to avoid importing the strategy package (which would create a cycle).
    19→const DefaultStrategyName = "manual-commit"
    20→
    21→const (
    22→	// EntireSettingsFile is the path to the Entire settings file
    23→	EntireSettingsFile = ".entire/settings.json"
    24→	// EntireSettingsLocalFile is the path to the local settings override file (not committed)
    25→	EntireSettingsLocalFile = ".entire/settings.local.json"
    26→)
    27→
    28→// EntireSettings represents the .entire/settings.json configuration
    29→type EntireSettings struct {
    30→	// Strategy is the name of the git strategy to use
    31→	Strategy string `json:"strategy"`
    32→
    33→	// Enabled indicates whether Entire is active. When false, CLI commands
    34→	// show a disabled message and hooks exit silently. Defaults to true.
    35→	Enabled bool `json:"enabled"`
    36→
    37→	// LocalDev indicates whether to use "go run" instead of the "entire" binary
    38→	// This is used for development when the binary is not installed
    39→	LocalDev bool `json:"local_dev,omitempty"`
    40→
    41→	// LogLevel sets the logging verbosity (debug, info, warn, error).
    42→	// Can be overridden by ENTIRE_LOG_LEVEL environment variable.
    43→	// Defaults to "info".
    44→	LogLevel string `json:"log_level,omitempty"`
    45→
    46→	// StrategyOptions contains strategy-specific configuration
    47→	StrategyOptions map[string]any `json:"strategy_options,omitempty"`
    48→
    49→	// Telemetry controls anonymous usage analytics.
    50→	// nil = not asked yet (show prompt), true = opted in, false = opted out
    51→	Telemetry *bool `json:"telemetry,omitempty"`
    52→}
    53→
    54→// Load loads the Entire settings from .entire/settings.json,
    55→// then applies any overrides from .entire/settings.local.json if it exists.
    56→// Returns default settings if neither file exists.
    57→// Works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository.
    58→func Load() (*EntireSettings, error) {
    59→	// Get absolute paths for settings files
    60→	settingsFileAbs, err := paths.AbsPath(EntireSettingsFile)
    61→	if err != nil {
    62→		settingsFileAbs = EntireSettingsFile // Fallback to relative
    63→	}
    64→	localSettingsFileAbs, err := paths.AbsPath(EntireSettingsLocalFile)
    65→	if err != nil {
    66→		localSettingsFileAbs = EntireSettingsLocalFile // Fallback to relative
    67→	}
    68→
    69→	// Load base settings
    70→	settings, err := loadFromFile(settingsFileAbs)
    71→	if err != nil {
    72→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("reading settings file: %w", err)
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	// Apply local overrides if they exist
    76→	localData, err := os.ReadFile(localSettingsFileAbs) //nolint:gosec // path is from AbsPath or constant
    77→	if err != nil {
    78→		if !os.IsNotExist(err) {
    79→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("reading local settings file: %w", err)
    80→		}
    81→		// Local file doesn't exist, continue without overrides
    82→	} else {
    83→		if err := mergeJSON(settings, localData); err != nil {
    84→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("merging local settings: %w", err)
    85→		}
    86→	}
    87→
    88→	applyDefaults(settings)
    89→
    90→	return settings, nil
    91→}
    92→
    93→// LoadFromFile loads settings from a specific file path without merging local overrides.
    94→// Returns default settings if the file doesn't exist.
    95→// Use this when you need to display individual settings files separately.
    96→func LoadFromFile(filePath string) (*EntireSettings, error) {
    97→	return loadFromFile(filePath)
    98→}
    99→
   100→// loadFromFile loads settings from a specific file path.
   101→// Returns default settings if the file doesn't exist.
   102→func loadFromFile(filePath string) (*EntireSettings, error) {
   103→	settings := &EntireSettings{
   104→		Strategy: DefaultStrategyName,
   105→		Enabled:  true, // Default to enabled
   106→	}
   107→
   108→	data, err := os.ReadFile(filePath) //nolint:gosec // path is from caller
   109→	if err != nil {
   110→		if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   111→			return settings, nil
   112→		}
   113→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("%w", err)
   114→	}
   115→
   116→	dec := json.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(data))
   117→	dec.DisallowUnknownFields()
   118→	if err := dec.Decode(settings); err != nil {
   119→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("parsing settings file: %w", err)
   120→	}
   121→	applyDefaults(settings)
   122→
   123→	return settings, nil
   124→}
   125→
   126→// mergeJSON merges JSON data into existing settings.
   127→// Only non-zero values from the JSON override existing settings.
   128→func mergeJSON(settings *EntireSettings, data []byte) error {
   129→	// First, validate that there are no unknown keys using strict decoding
   130→	dec := json.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(data))
   131→	dec.DisallowUnknownFields()
   132→	var temp EntireSettings
   133→	if err := dec.Decode(&temp); err != nil {
   134→		return fmt.Errorf("parsing JSON: %w", err)
   135→	}
   136→
   137→	// Parse into a map to check which fields are present
   138→	var raw map[string]json.RawMessage
   139→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &raw); err != nil {
   140→		return fmt.Errorf("parsing JSON: %w", err)
   141→	}
   142→
   143→	// Override strategy if present and non-empty
   144→	if strategyRaw, ok := raw["strategy"]; ok {
   145→		var s string
   146→		if err := json.Unmarshal(strategyRaw, &s); err != nil {
   147→			return fmt.Errorf("parsing strategy field: %w", err)
   148→		}
   149→		if s != "" {
   150→			settings.Strategy = s
   151→		}
   152→	}
   153→
   154→	// Override enabled if present
   155→	if enabledRaw, ok := raw["enabled"]; ok {
   156→		var e bool
   157→		if err := json.Unmarshal(enabledRaw, &e); err != nil {
   158→			return fmt.Errorf("parsing enabled field: %w", err)
   159→		}
   160→		settings.Enabled = e
   161→	}
   162→
   163→	// Override local_dev if present
   164→	if localDevRaw, ok := raw["local_dev"]; ok {
   165→		var ld bool
   166→		if err := json.Unmarshal(localDevRaw, &ld); err != nil {
   167→			return fmt.Errorf("parsing local_dev field: %w", err)
   168→		}
   169→		settings.LocalDev = ld
   170→	}
   171→
   172→	// Override log_level if present and non-empty
   173→	if logLevelRaw, ok := raw["log_level"]; ok {
   174→		var ll string
   175→		if err := json.Unmarshal(logLevelRaw, &ll); err != nil {
   176→			return fmt.Errorf("parsing log_level field: %w", err)
   177→		}
   178→		if ll != "" {
   179→			settings.LogLevel = ll
   180→		}
   181→	}
   182→
   183→	// Merge strategy_options if present
   184→	if optionsRaw, ok := raw["strategy_options"]; ok {
   185→		var opts map[string]any
   186→		if err := json.Unmarshal(optionsRaw, &opts); err != nil {
   187→			return fmt.Errorf("parsing strategy_options field: %w", err)
   188→		}
   189→		if settings.StrategyOptions == nil {
   190→			settings.StrategyOptions = opts
   191→		} else {
   192→			for k, v := range opts {
   193→				settings.StrategyOptions[k] = v
   194→			}
   195→		}
   196→	}
   197→
   198→	// Override telemetry if present
   199→	if telemetryRaw, ok := raw["telemetry"]; ok {
   200→		var t bool
   201→		if err := json.Unmarshal(telemetryRaw, &t); err != nil {
   202→			return fmt.Errorf("parsing telemetry field: %w", err)
   203→		}
   204→		settings.Telemetry = &t
   205→	}
   206→
   207→	return nil
   208→}
   209→
   210→func applyDefaults(settings *EntireSettings) {
   211→	if settings.Strategy == "" {
   212→		settings.Strategy = DefaultStrategyName
   213→	}
   214→}
   215→
   216→// IsSummarizeEnabled checks if auto-summarize is enabled in settings.
   217→// Returns false by default if settings cannot be loaded or the key is missing.
   218→func IsSummarizeEnabled() bool {
   219→	settings, err := Load()
   220→	if err != nil {
   221→		return false
   222→	}
   223→	return settings.IsSummarizeEnabled()
   224→}
   225→
   226→// IsSummarizeEnabled checks if auto-summarize is enabled in this settings instance.
   227→func (s *EntireSettings) IsSummarizeEnabled() bool {
   228→	if s.StrategyOptions == nil {
   229→		return false
   230→	}
   231→	summarizeOpts, ok := s.StrategyOptions["summarize"].(map[string]any)
   232→	if !ok {
   233→		return false
   234→	}
   235→	enabled, ok := summarizeOpts["enabled"].(bool)
   236→	if !ok {
   237→		return false
   238→	}
   239→	return enabled
   240→}
   241→
   242→// IsPushSessionsDisabled checks if push_sessions is disabled in settings.
   243→// Returns true if push_sessions is explicitly set to false.
   244→func (s *EntireSettings) IsPushSessionsDisabled() bool {
   245→	if s.StrategyOptions == nil {
   246→		return false
   247→	}
   248→	val, exists := s.StrategyOptions["push_sessions"]
   249→	if !exists {
   250→		return false
   251→	}
   252→	if boolVal, ok := val.(bool); ok {
   253→		return !boolVal // disabled = !push_sessions
   254→	}
   255→	return false
   256→}
   257→
   258→// Save saves the settings to .entire/settings.json.
   259→func Save(settings *EntireSettings) error {
   260→	return saveToFile(settings, EntireSettingsFile)
   261→}
   262→
   263→// SaveLocal saves the settings to .entire/settings.local.json.
   264→func SaveLocal(settings *EntireSettings) error {
   265→	return saveToFile(settings, EntireSettingsLocalFile)
   266→}
   267→
   268→// saveToFile saves settings to the specified file path.
   269→func saveToFile(settings *EntireSettings, filePath string) error {
   270→	// Get absolute path for the file
   271→	filePathAbs, err := paths.AbsPath(filePath)
   272→	if err != nil {
   273→		filePathAbs = filePath // Fallback to relative
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	// Ensure directory exists
   277→	dir := filepath.Dir(filePathAbs)
   278→	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o750); err != nil {
   279→		return fmt.Errorf("creating settings directory: %w", err)
   280→	}
   281→
   282→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(settings, "", "  ")
   283→	if err != nil {
   284→		return fmt.Errorf("marshaling settings: %w", err)
   285→	}
   286→
   287→	//nolint:gosec // G306: settings file is config, not secrets; 0o644 is appropriate
   288→	if err := os.WriteFile(filePathAbs, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   289→		return fmt.Errorf("writing settings file: %w", err)
   290→	}
   291→	return nil
   292→}
   293→
```

        GetHooksDir()                   1× rev-parse      0                     git-path hooks
        hookCmdPrefix() → isLocalDev()
          settings.Load()              0                 2 reads               REDUNDANT #1
                                        0                 4 read + 4 write      hook files (read+write each)

        paths.RepoRoot()               0                 (cached)
        detectHookManagers()            0                 ~20 stats             hook manager configs
        hookCmdPrefix() → isLocalDev()
          settings.Load()              0                 2 reads               REDUNDANT #2
795   strategy.Get()                    0                 0                     in-memory registry
799   strat.EnsureSetup()
        EnsureEntireGitignore()         0                 1 read                REDUNDANT #3
        OpenRepository()

          git.PlainOpen()               0                 several reads         .git dir
        EnsureMetadataBranch()          0                 go-git I/O            creates orphan branch
        IsGitHookInstalled()
          GetHooksDir()                 1× rev-parse      0                     REDUNDANT #5
          read 4 hook files             0                 4 reads               REDUNDANT #6 (just installed!)

```

**Clear redundancies worth fixing:**

| # | What | Cost | Fix |
|---|------|------|-----|
| 1-2 | `settings.Load()` called 2× inside `InstallGitHook` and `CheckAndWarnHookManagers` via `isLocalDev()` | 4 file reads + JSON parse each | Caller already knows `localDev` — pass it through |
| 3 | `EnsureEntireGitignore()` in `EnsureSetup()` | 1 file read | `setupEntireDirectory()` already called it 10 lines above |
| 4 | `GetWorktreePath()` subprocess in `OpenRepository()` | 1 subprocess spawn | `paths.RepoRoot()` cache is already warm — use it |
| 5-6 | `IsGitHookInstalled()` + `GetHooksDir()` in `EnsureSetup()` | 1 subprocess + 4 file reads | We literally just called `InstallGitHook` — hooks are installed |

These are all straightforward: no behavioral changes, no new abstractions, just avoiding re-doing work the caller already did. The subprocess spawns (~2ms each on macOS) and redundant `settings.Load()` calls (2 file reads + JSON parse each) are the most impactful.

Want me to go ahead and fix these? The approach for each:
1. **settings.Load() redundancy** — pass `localDev bool` into `InstallGitHook` and `CheckAndWarnHookManagers`
2. **OpenRepository()** — swap `GetWorktreePath()` for `paths.RepoRoot()` (same result, cached)
3. **EnsureSetup() redundancy** — add an `EnsureSetupWithOpts` that lets callers skip work already done

Here's the complete evaluation:

## Call Site Analysis for `localDev` Parameter

### `InstallGitHook(silent bool)` — 6 production call sites

| Call site | Has `localDev`? | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `setup.go:269` — `runEnableWithStrategy()` | Yes | param available |
| `setup.go:355` — `runEnableInteractive()` | Yes | param available |
| `setup.go:773` — `setupAgentHooksNonInteractive()` | Yes | param available |
| `setup.go:875` — `setupGitHook()` | No | Hidden command, rarely used |
| `manual_commit.go:115` — `EnsureSetup()` | No | Called from hooks/lifecycle |
| `auto_commit.go:479` — `EnsureSetup()` | No | Called from hooks/lifecycle |

### `CheckAndWarnHookManagers(w)` — 4 production call sites

| Call site | Has `localDev`? | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `setup.go:272` — `runEnableWithStrategy()` | Yes | |
| `setup.go:358` — `runEnableInteractive()` | Yes | |
| `setup.go:776` — `setupAgentHooksNonInteractive()` | Yes | |
| `setup.go:879` — `setupGitHook()` | No | Hidden command |

### Key Findings

The 3 call sites that **don't** have `localDev` are:

1. **`setupGitHook()`** — a hidden standalone command (`entire setup git-hook`). Rarely called, not a hot path. It can simply call `settings.Load()` itself to get the value.

2. **`EnsureSetup()` on both strategies** — called from `lifecycle.go:136` (hook event handlers) and from `setup.go:277,382,799` (enable paths). In the `EnsureSetup()` case, `InstallGitHook` is only called as a **self-healing fallback** when `IsGitHookInstalled()` returns false — meaning hooks were removed externally. This is rare and not performance-critical.

### Recommended Approach

Add `localDev bool` parameter to both `InstallGitHook` and `CheckAndWarnHookManagers`. For the 3 call sites that don't have it available:

- **`setupGitHook()`**: Call `isLocalDev()` once at the top and pass it to both functions.
- **`EnsureSetup()` (both strategies)**: Call `isLocalDev()` inline. This is the self-healing path that rarely fires, so the extra `settings.Load()` there is fine.

This eliminates the redundant `settings.Load()` on the **hot path** (enable commands) while keeping the cold paths simple. Want me to proceed with this implementation?

   165→// InstallGitHook installs generic git hooks that delegate to `entire hook` commands.
   166→// These hooks work with any strategy - the strategy is determined at runtime.
   167→// If silent is true, no output is printed (except backup notifications, which always print).
   168→// Returns the number of hooks that were installed (0 if all already up to date).
   169→func InstallGitHook(silent bool) (int, error) {
   170→	hooksDir, err := GetHooksDir()
   171→	if err != nil {
   172→		return 0, err
   173→	}
   174→
   175→	if err := os.MkdirAll(hooksDir, 0o755); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // Git hooks require executable permissions
   176→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to create hooks directory: %w", err)
   177→	}
   178→
   179→	specs := buildHookSpecs(hookCmdPrefix())
   180→	installedCount := 0
   181→
   182→	for _, spec := range specs {
   183→		hookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, spec.name)
   184→		backupPath := hookPath + backupSuffix
   185→		backupExists := fileExists(backupPath)
   186→
   187→		// Back up existing non-Entire hooks
   188→		existing, existingErr := os.ReadFile(hookPath) //nolint:gosec // path is controlled
   189→		if existingErr == nil && !strings.Contains(string(existing), entireHookMarker) {
   190→			if !backupExists {
   191→				if err := os.Rename(hookPath, backupPath); err != nil {
   192→					return installedCount, fmt.Errorf("failed to back up %s: %w", spec.name, err)
   193→				}
   194→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Backed up existing %s to %s%s\n", spec.name, spec.name, backupSuffix)
   195→			} else {
   196→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: replacing %s (backup %s%s already exists from a previous install)\n", spec.name, spec.name, backupSuffix)
   197→			}
   198→			backupExists = true
   199→		}
   200→
   201→		// Chain to backup if one exists
   202→		content := spec.content
   203→		if backupExists {
   204→			content = generateChainedContent(spec.content, spec.name)
   205→		}
   206→
   207→		written, err := writeHookFile(hookPath, content)
   208→		if err != nil {
   209→			return installedCount, fmt.Errorf("failed to install %s hook: %w", spec.name, err)
   210→		}
   211→		if written {
   212→			installedCount++
   213→		}
   214→	}
   215→
   216→	if !silent {
   217→		fmt.Println("✓ Installed git hooks (prepare-commit-msg, commit-msg, post-commit, pre-push)")
   218→		fmt.Println("  Hooks delegate to the current strategy at runtime")
   219→	}
   220→
   221→	return installedCount, nil
   222→}
   223→
   224→// writeHookFile writes a hook file if it doesn't exist or has different content.
```

  "old_string": "// InstallGitHook installs generic git hooks that delegate to `entire hook` commands.\n// These hooks work with any strategy - the strategy is determined at runtime.\n// If silent is true, no output is printed (except backup notifications, which always print).\n// Returns the number of hooks that were installed (0 if all already up to date).\nfunc InstallGitHook(silent bool) (int, error) {\n\thooksDir, err := GetHooksDir()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn 0, err\n\t}\n\n\tif err := os.MkdirAll(hooksDir, 0o755); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // Git hooks require executable permissions\n\t\treturn 0, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create hooks directory: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tspecs := buildHookSpecs(hookCmdPrefix())",
  "new_string": "// InstallGitHook installs generic git hooks that delegate to `entire hook` commands.\n// These hooks work with any strategy - the strategy is determined at runtime.\n// If silent is true, no output is printed (except backup notifications, which always print).\n// localDev controls whether hooks use \"go run\" (true) or the \"entire\" binary (false).\n// Returns the number of hooks that were installed (0 if all already up to date).\nfunc InstallGitHook(silent bool, localDev bool) (int, error) {\n\thooksDir, err := GetHooksDir()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn 0, err\n\t}\n\n\tif err := os.MkdirAll(hooksDir, 0o755); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // Git hooks require executable permissions\n\t\treturn 0, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create hooks directory: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tspecs := buildHookSpecs(hookCmdPrefix(localDev))"
}

> AGENT

Done. Here's what changed:

**`strategy/hooks.go`** — `hookCmdPrefix()` now takes `localDev bool` instead of calling `isLocalDev()` internally. `InstallGitHook()` takes a new `localDev bool` parameter and passes it through. `isLocalDev()` is kept for the self-healing paths.

**`strategy/hook_managers.go`** — `CheckAndWarnHookManagers()` takes a new `localDev bool` parameter.

**`setup.go`** — The 3 hot-path call sites (`runEnableWithStrategy`, `runEnableInteractive`, `setupAgentHooksNonInteractive`) pass their existing `localDev` variable directly. `setupGitHook()` (hidden command, cold path) loads settings once and passes through.

**`strategy/manual_commit.go` + `auto_commit.go`** — `EnsureSetup()` (self-healing fallback, only fires when hooks are externally deleted) calls `isLocalDev()` inline.

**Test files** — All `InstallGitHook(true)` → `InstallGitHook(true, false)` and `CheckAndWarnHookManagers(&buf)` → `CheckAndWarnHookManagers(&buf, false)`.

On the hot path, this eliminates 2-3 redundant `settings.Load()` calls (each reading 2 JSON files + JSON parse).

> DEVELOPER

### **Optimization 1: Cache GetHooksDir()**

#### Current Implementation
- **Function definition**: `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/hooks.go:46-48`

   - `IsGitHookInstalled()` calls `GetHooksDir()`

2. `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/hooks.go:171`
   - `InstallGitHook()` calls `GetHooksDir()`

3. `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/hooks.go:245`

**Key Call Chain in setupAgentHooksNonInteractive:**
- Line 771: `strategy.InstallGitHook()` calls `GetHooksDir()` (line 171 in hooks.go)
- Line 797: `strat.EnsureSetup()` indirectly calls `IsGitHookInstalled()` → `GetHooksDir()` (line 95 in hooks.go)
  - Manual-commit: `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit.go:114` calls `IsGitHookInstalled()`

**In test code:**

#### **Existing Caching Pattern**

Reference: `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths.go:49-102`

The `paths.RepoRoot()` function uses this caching pattern:
```go
var (
    repoRootMu       sync.RWMutex
    repoRootCache    string
    repoRootCacheDir string  // Cache key: current working directory
)

func RepoRoot() (string, error) {
    cwd, err := os.Getwd()
    if err != nil {
        cwd = ""
    }
    
    // Check cache with read lock
    repoRootMu.RLock()
    if repoRootCache != "" && repoRootCacheDir == cwd {
        cached := repoRootCache
        repoRootMu.RUnlock()
        return cached, nil
    }
    repoRootMu.RUnlock()
    
    // Cache miss - execute command and update with write lock
    // ... git command ...
    
    repoRootMu.Lock()
    repoRootCache = root
    repoRootCacheDir = cwd
    repoRootMu.Unlock()
    
    return root, nil
}

// Helper to clear cache (for testing)
func ClearRepoRootCache() { ... }
```

**Key characteristics:**
- Uses CWD as cache key (handles directory changes)
- RWMutex for thread-safe reads and writes
- Separate function `ClearRepoRootCache()` for test cleanup

### **Optimization 2: Make OpenRepository() Use paths.RepoRoot()**

#### Current Implementation

**Function definition**: `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/common.go:560-577`

```go
func OpenRepository() (*git.Repository, error) {
    // Line 563: Calls GetWorktreePath() - spawns subprocess
    repoRoot, err := GetWorktreePath()
    if err != nil {
        repoRoot = "."
    }
    
    repo, err := git.PlainOpenWithOptions(repoRoot, &git.PlainOpenOptions{
        EnableDotGitCommonDir: true,
    })
    if err != nil {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open repository: %w", err)
    }
    return repo, nil
}
```

**GetWorktreePath implementation**: `/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/common.go:659-669`

```go
func GetWorktreePath() (string, error) {
    ctx := context.Background()
    cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel")  // Line 663: subprocess
    output, err := cmd.Output()
    if err != nil {
        return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get worktree path: %w", err)
    }
    return strings.TrimSpace(string(output)), nil
}
```

**Key Observation**: Both `GetWorktreePath()` and `paths.RepoRoot()` execute the same git command (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`), but:
- `GetWorktreePath()` has **no caching**
- `paths.RepoRoot()` has **CWD-keyed RWMutex caching**

Benchmark results (3 runs): **~42ms NewRepo, ~38ms ReEnable** — essentially the same as baseline. The `settings.Load()` savings are small individually but eliminate unnecessary file I/O.

## Recommended Next 2 Optimizations

Based on the audit, these two give the biggest bang for the buck:

### 1. Cache `GetHooksDir()` result
`GetHooksDir()` spawns `git rev-parse --git-path hooks` and is called **2x** on the enable path: once in `InstallGitHook()` and once in `IsGitHookInstalled()` via `EnsureSetup()`. Each call is a subprocess spawn. We can add CWD-keyed caching matching the existing `paths.RepoRoot()` pattern.

### 2. Make `OpenRepository()` use `paths.RepoRoot()` instead of `GetWorktreePath()`
`OpenRepository()` calls `GetWorktreePath()` which spawns `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` — the exact same command that `paths.RepoRoot()` runs, except `paths.RepoRoot()` caches the result. By the time `OpenRepository()` runs in the enable flow, the cache is already warm from earlier in the path. This is a one-line change in `OpenRepository()`.

Both eliminate subprocess spawns, which are the most expensive operations in this path. Want me to proceed with both?

    56→	output, err := cmd.Output()
    57→	if err != nil {
    58→		return "", errors.New("not a git repository")
    59→	}
    60→
    61→	gitDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
    62→
    63→	// git rev-parse --git-dir returns relative paths from the working directory,
    64→	// so we need to make it absolute if it isn't already
    65→	if !filepath.IsAbs(gitDir) {
    66→		gitDir = filepath.Join(dir, gitDir)
    67→	}
    68→
    69→	return filepath.Clean(gitDir), nil
    70→}
    71→
    72→// getHooksDirInPath returns the active hooks directory for a repository at the given path.
    73→// It delegates to `git rev-parse --git-path hooks` so Git resolves:
    74→// - linked-worktree common hooks directory
    75→// - core.hooksPath (relative or absolute)
    76→func getHooksDirInPath(dir string) (string, error) {
    77→	ctx := context.Background()
    78→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--git-path", "hooks")
    79→	cmd.Dir = dir
    80→	output, err := cmd.Output()
    81→	if err != nil {
    82→		return "", errors.New("not a git repository")
    83→	}
    84→
    85→	hooksDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
    86→	if !filepath.IsAbs(hooksDir) {
    87→		hooksDir = filepath.Join(dir, hooksDir)
    88→	}
    89→
    90→	return filepath.Clean(hooksDir), nil
    91→}
    92→
    93→// IsGitHookInstalled checks if all generic Entire CLI hooks are installed.
    94→func IsGitHookInstalled() bool {
    95→	hooksDir, err := GetHooksDir()
    96→	if err != nil {
    97→		return false
    98→	}
    99→	return isGitHookInstalledInHooksDir(hooksDir)
   100→}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/hooks.go",
  "old_string": "// GetHooksDir returns the active hooks directory path.\n// This respects core.hooksPath and correctly resolves to the common hooks\n// directory when called from a linked worktree.\nfunc GetHooksDir() (string, error) {\n\treturn getHooksDirInPath(\".\")\n}",
  "new_string": "// hooksDirCache caches the hooks directory to avoid repeated git subprocess spawns.\n// Keyed by current working directory to handle directory changes.\nvar (\n\thooksDirMu       sync.RWMutex\n\thooksDirCache    string\n\thooksDirCacheDir string\n)\n\n// GetHooksDir returns the active hooks directory path.\n// This respects core.hooksPath and correctly resolves to the common hooks\n// directory when called from a linked worktree.\n// The result is cached per working directory.\nfunc GetHooksDir() (string, error) {\n\tcwd, err := os.Getwd()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tcwd = \"\"\n\t}\n\n\thooksDirMu.RLock()\n\tif hooksDirCache != \"\" && hooksDirCacheDir == cwd {\n\t\tcached := hooksDirCache\n\t\thooksDirMu.RUnlock()\n\t\treturn cached, nil\n\t}\n\thooksDirMu.RUnlock()\n\n\tresult, err := getHooksDirInPath(\".\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\n\thooksDirMu.Lock()\n\thooksDirCache = result\n\thooksDirCacheDir = cwd\n\thooksDirMu.Unlock()\n\n\treturn result, nil\n}\n\n// ClearHooksDirCache clears the cached hooks directory.\n// This is primarily useful for testing when changing directories.\nfunc ClearHooksDirCache() {\n\thooksDirMu.Lock()\n\thooksDirCache = \"\"\n\thooksDirCacheDir = \"\"\n\thooksDirMu.Unlock()\n}"
}

   557→//
   558→// The function first uses 'git rev-parse --show-toplevel' to find the repository
   559→// root, which works correctly even when called from a subdirectory within the repo.
   560→func OpenRepository() (*git.Repository, error) {
   561→	// First, find the repository root using git rev-parse --show-toplevel
   562→	// This works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository
   563→	repoRoot, err := GetWorktreePath()
   564→	if err != nil {
   565→		// Fallback to current directory if git command fails
   566→		// (e.g., if git is not installed or we're not in a repo)
   567→		repoRoot = "."
   568→	}
   569→
   570→	repo, err := git.PlainOpenWithOptions(repoRoot, &git.PlainOpenOptions{
   571→		EnableDotGitCommonDir: true,
   572→	})
   573→	if err != nil {
   574→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open repository: %w", err)
   575→	}
   576→	return repo, nil
   577→}
   578→

   580→// (as opposed to the main repository). Worktrees have .git as a file pointing
   581→// to the main repo, while the main repo has .git as a directory.
   582→// This function works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository.

> AGENT

Here's the evaluation:

**Functional comparison:**

| | `GetWorktreePath()` | `paths.RepoRoot()` |
|---|---|---|
| Command | `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` | `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` |
| Caching | None | CWD-keyed RWMutex |
| Error message | `"failed to get worktree path"` | `"failed to get git repository root"` |
| Return | `strings.TrimSpace(output)` | `strings.TrimSpace(output)` |

They run the **exact same git command** and process the output identically. The only differences are the error message string and caching.

**Edge case: subdirectories.** Both use `--show-toplevel` without setting `cmd.Dir`, so both resolve from the current working directory. Identical behavior.

**Edge case: error handling in `OpenRepository()`.** Currently if `GetWorktreePath()` fails, it falls back to `repoRoot = "."`. With `paths.RepoRoot()`, the error message changes but the fallback logic stays the same. No behavioral difference.

**Edge case: worktrees.** `--show-toplevel` returns the worktree root in both cases, not the main repo root. Both are equivalent.

There are no functional differences — they're the same command with/without caching. The change is safe. Let me also check if `GetWorktreePath()` has other callers that should stay untouched:

> DEVELOPER

we have some feedback to update:

The parse_benchmarks function stores results in a dict keyed by benchmark name, overwriting the value each iteration. With BENCH_COUNT defaulting to 6, each benchmark produces 6 output lines, but results[name] = (ns / 1e6, bop, allocs) keeps only the last one. This discards 5 of 6 data points, making the comparison unreliable — the whole point of running multiple iterations is statistical robustness. The previous benchstat tool properly aggregated all runs.

The EXIT trap was changed to only rm -rf "$tmpdir", removing the git branch and stash restoration that the old trap performed. If the script is interrupted (e.g., Ctrl+C) while running benchmarks on the base ref, the user is left on the wrong branch with their uncommitted changes stashed and no indication of how to recover.

\"$new_out\" <<'PYEOF'\nimport sys, statistics\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\nresults = defaultdict(lambda: {\"ns\": [], \"bop\": [], \"allocs\": []})\nfor line in open(sys.argv[1]):\n    if not line.startswith(\"Benchmark\"):\n        continue\n    parts = line.split()\n    if len(parts) < 8:\n        continue\n    try:\n        name = parts[0]\n        results[name][\"ns\"].append(float(parts[2]))\n        results[name][\"bop\"].append(int(parts[4]))\n        results[name][\"allocs\"].append(int(parts[6]))\n    except (ValueError, IndexError):\n        continue\n\nprint(f\"  {'Benchmark':<43} {'ms/op':>8} {'B/op':>8} {'allocs/op':>9}\")\nprint(f\"  {'─'*43} {'─'*8} {'─'*8} {'─'*9}\")\nfor name, v in results.items():\n    short = name.replace(\"BenchmarkEnableCommand/\", \"\").replace(\"Benchmark\", \"\")\n    ms = statistics.median(v[\"ns\"]) / 1e6\n    bop = int(statistics.median(v[\"bop\"]))\n    allocs = int(statistics.median(v[\"allocs\"]))\n    n = len(v[\"ns\"])\n    print(f\"  {short:<43} {ms:>6.1f}ms {bop:>8} {allocs:>9}  (n={n})\")\nPYEOF\n  exit 0\nfi\n\ngit checkout \"$current_branch\" --quiet 2>/dev/null\non_base_ref=false\n[ \"$has_changes\" = true ] && git stash pop --quiet && has_changes=false\n\n# Both branches have results — show comparison table (median of all runs)\necho \"\"\npython3 - o_ms) / o_ms * 100) if o_ms > 0 else 0\n        d_b = ((n_b - o_b) / o_b * 100) if o_b > 0 else 0\n        d_a = ((n_a - o_a) / o_a * 100) if o_a > 0 else 0\n        print(f\"  {short:<43} {o_ms:>6.1f} → {n_ms:>6.1f} {fmt_delta(d_ms):>7}  {o_b:>7} → {n_b:>7} {fmt_delta(d_b):>7}  {o_a:>5} → {n_a:>5} {fmt_delta(d_a):>7}\")\n    elif name in new:\n        n_ms, n_b, n_a, n_n = new[name]\n        print(f\"  {short:<43} {'':>6}   {n_ms:>6.1f} {'(new)':>7}  {'':>7}   {n_b:>7} {'(new)':>7}  {'':>5}   {n_a:>5} {'(new)':>7}\")\n\nn = next((v[3] for v in new.values()), 0)\nprint()\nprint(f\"  base: {base_name}    current: {curr_name}    (median of {n} runs)\")\nPYEOF\n\"\"\""

> AGENT

Done. Two fixes:

1. **Median aggregation**: `parse_benchmarks()` now collects all N runs per benchmark into lists and computes the median for each metric. The output also shows `(median of N runs)` so it's clear what you're looking at.

2. **Safe cleanup trap**: Tracks `on_base_ref` and `has_changes` state flags. The `cleanup` function in the EXIT trap restores the original branch and pops the stash if interrupted at any point — whether by Ctrl+C, a failed benchmark, or any other error.

3. **Transition**: verifies switching from true→false correctly reinstalls hooks (count > 0, content updated)

   126→run = """

   129→

   131→BENCH_PKG="${BENCH_PKG:-./...}"
   132→BENCH_COUNT="${BENCH_COUNT:-6}"
   133→BENCH_TIMEOUT="${BENCH_TIMEOUT:-10m}"
   134→BASE_REF="${BASE_REF:-main}"
   135→
   136→current_branch=$(git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD)
   137→if [ "$current_branch" = "$BASE_REF" ]; then
   138→  echo "Already on $BASE_REF — nothing to compare. Run from a feature branch."
   139→  exit 1
   140→fi
   141→
   142→tmpdir=$(mktemp -d)
   143→on_base_ref=false
   144→has_changes=false
   145→
   146→cleanup() {
   147→  if [ "$on_base_ref" = true ]; then
   148→    git checkout "$current_branch" --quiet 2>/dev/null || true
   149→  fi
   150→  if [ "$has_changes" = true ]; then
   151→    git stash pop --quiet 2>/dev/null || true
   152→  fi
   153→  rm -rf "$tmpdir"
   154→}
   155→trap cleanup EXIT
   156→
   157→run_bench() {
   158→  local label="$1" out="$2"
   159→  echo "=== Benchmarking: $label ==="
   160→  go test -bench="$BENCH_PATTERN" -benchmem -run='^$' -count="$BENCH_COUNT" -timeout="$BENCH_TIMEOUT" "$BENCH_PKG" 2>/dev/null \
   161→    | grep -E '^(Benchmark|goos:|goarch:|pkg:|cpu:)' > "$out"
   162→  local count
   163→  count=$(grep -c '^Benchmark' "$out" || true)
   164→  if [ "$count" -eq 0 ]; then
   165→    echo "  ERROR: no benchmark results captured. Does the benchmark exist on this branch?"
   166→    return 1
   167→  fi
   168→  echo "  captured $count benchmark lines"
   169→}
   170→
   171→# Run on current branch first
   172→new_out="$tmpdir/new.txt"
   173→if ! run_bench "$current_branch" "$new_out"; then
   174→  exit 1
   175→fi
   176→
   177→# Stash if needed, switch to base, run, switch back
   178→if ! git diff --quiet || ! git diff --cached --quiet; then
   179→  has_changes=true
   180→  git stash push -q -m "bench:compare auto-stash"
   181→fi
   182→
   183→old_out="$tmpdir/old.txt"
   184→echo ""
   185→git checkout "$BASE_REF" --quiet 2>/dev/null
   186→on_base_ref=true
   187→if ! run_bench "$BASE_REF" "$old_out"; then
   188→  echo ""
   189→  echo "Benchmark does not exist on $BASE_REF. Showing current branch results only:"
   190→  echo ""
   191→  git checkout "$current_branch" --quiet 2>/dev/null
   192→  on_base_ref=false
   193→  [ "$has_changes" = true ] && git stash pop --quiet && has_changes=false
   194→
   195→  # Pretty-print single-branch results (aggregate median across runs)
   196→  python3 - "$new_out" <<'PYEOF'
   197→import sys, statistics
   198→from collections import defaultdict
   199→
   200→results = defaultdict(lambda: {"ns": [], "bop": [], "allocs": []})
   201→for line in open(sys.argv[1]):
   202→    if not line.startswith("Benchmark"):
   203→        continue
   204→    parts = line.split()
   205→    if len(parts) < 8:
   206→        continue
   207→    try:
   208→        name = parts[0]
   209→        results[name]["ns"].append(float(parts[2]))
   210→        results[name]["bop"].append(int(parts[4]))
   211→        results[name]["allocs"].append(int(parts[6]))
   212→    except (ValueError, IndexError):
   213→        continue
   214→
   215→print(f"  {'Benchmark':<43} {'ms/op':>8} {'B/op':>8} {'allocs/op':>9}")
   216→print(f"  {'─'*43} {'─'*8} {'─'*8} {'─'*9}")
   217→for name, v in results.items():
   218→    short = name.replace("BenchmarkEnableCommand/", "").replace("Benchmark", "")
   219→    ms = statistics.median(v["ns"]) / 1e6
   220→    bop = int(statistics.median(v["bop"]))
   221→    allocs = int(statistics.median(v["allocs"]))
   222→    n = len(v["ns"])
   223→    print(f"  {short:<43} {ms:>6.1f}ms {bop:>8} {allocs:>9}  (n={n})")
   224→PYEOF
   225→  exit 0
   226→fi
   227→
   228→git checkout "$current_branch" --quiet 2>/dev/null
   229→on_base_ref=false
   230→[ "$has_changes" = true ] && git stash pop --quiet && has_changes=false
   231→
   232→# Both branches have results — show comparison table (median of all runs)
   233→echo ""
   234→python3 - "$old_out" "$new_out" "$BASE_REF" "$current_branch" <<'PYEOF'
   235→import sys, statistics
   236→from collections import defaultdict
   237→
   238→old_file, new_file, base_name, curr_name = sys.argv[1:5]
   239→
   240→def parse_benchmarks(path):
   241→    raw = defaultdict(lambda: {"ns": [], "bop": [], "allocs": []})
   242→    for line in open(path):
   243→        if not line.startswith("Benchmark"):

There it is — lines 6 and 8 start with `BenchmarkEnableCommand/` followed by `✓` instead of numbers. The grep catches them because they start with `Benchmark`. I need to also require a tab+digits after the benchmark name. Let me use a tighter pattern:

id: toolu_018b1AuvVKBaeztMCyLLfyob
```
Usage: benchstat [flags] inputs...

benchstat computes statistical summaries and A/B comparisons of Go
benchmarks. It shows benchmark medians in a table with a row for each
benchmark and a column for each input file. If there is more than one
input file, it also shows A/B comparisons between the files. If a
difference is likely to be noise, it shows "~".

For details, see https://pkg.go.dev/golang.org/x/perf/cmd/benchstat.
  -alpha α
    	consider change significant if p < α (default 0.05)
  -col projection
    	split results into columns by distinct values of projection (default ".file")
  -confidence level
    	confidence level for ranges (default 0.95)
  -filter query
    	use only benchmarks matching benchfilter query (default "*")
  -format format
    	print results in format:
    	  text - plain text
    	  csv  - comma-separated values (warnings will be written to stderr)
    	 (default "text")
  -ignore keys
    	ignore variations in keys
  -row projection
    	split results into rows by distinct values of projection (default ".fullname")
  -table projection
    	split results into tables by distinct values of projection (default ".config")
```

\"$new_out\" <<'PYEOF'\nimport sys, statistics\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\nresults = defaultdict(lambda: {\"ns\": [], \"bop\": [], \"allocs\": []})\nfor line in open(sys.argv[1]):\n    if not line.startswith(\"Benchmark\"):\n        continue\n    parts = line.split()\n    if len(parts) < 8:\n        continue\n    try:\n        name = parts[0]\n        results[name][\"ns\"].append(float(parts[2]))\n        results[name][\"bop\"].append(int(parts[4]))\n        results[name][\"allocs\"].append(int(parts[6]))\n    except (ValueError, IndexError):\n        continue\n\nprint(f\"  {'Benchmark':<43} {'ms/op':>8} {'B/op':>8} {'allocs/op':>9}\")\nprint(f\"  {'─'*43} {'─'*8} {'─'*8} {'─'*9}\")\nfor name, v in results.items():\n    short = name.replace(\"BenchmarkEnableCommand/\", \"\").replace(\"Benchmark\", \"\")\n    ms = statistics.median(v[\"ns\"]) / 1e6\n    bop = int(statistics.median(v[\"bop\"]))\n    allocs = int(statistics.median(v[\"allocs\"]))\n    n = len(v[\"ns\"])\n    print(f\"  {short:<43} {ms:>6.1f}ms {bop:>8} {allocs:>9}  (n={n})\")\nPYEOF\n  exit 0\nfi\n\ngit checkout \"$current_branch\" --quiet 2>/dev/null\non_base_ref=false\n[ \"$has_changes\" = true ] && git stash pop --quiet && has_changes=false\n\n# Both branches have results — show comparison table (median of all runs)\necho \"\"\npython3 - \"$old_out\" \"$new_out\" \"$BASE_REF\" \"$current_branch\" <<'PYEOF'\nimport sys, statistics\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\nold_file, new_file, base_name, curr_name = sys.argv[1:5]\n\ndef parse_benchmarks(path):\n    raw = defaultdict(lambda: {\"ns\": [], \"bop\": [], \"allocs\": []})\n    for line in open(path):\n        if not line.startswith(\"Benchmark\"):\n            continue\n        parts = line.split()\n        if len(parts) < 8:\n            continue\n        try:\n            name = parts[0]\n            raw[name][\"ns\"].append(float(parts[2]))\n            raw[name][\"bop\"].append(int(parts[4]))\n            raw[name][\"allocs\"].append(int(parts[6]))\n        except (ValueError, IndexError):\n            continue\n    return {\n        name: (\n            statistics.median(v[\"ns\"]) / 1e6,\n            int(statistics.median(v[\"bop\"])),\n            int(statistics.median(v[\"allocs\"])),\n            len(v[\"ns\"]),\n        )\n        for name, v in raw.items()\n    }\n\nold = parse_benchmarks(old_file)\nnew = parse_benchmarks(new_file)\nall_names = list(dict.fromkeys(list(new.keys()) + list(old.keys())))\n\nprint(f\"  {'Benchmark':<43} {'ms/op':>18} {'B/op':>18} {'allocs/op':>18}\")\nprint(f\"  {'─'*43}─{'─'*18}─{'─'*18}─{'─'*18}\")\n\ndef fmt_delta(d):\n    return f\"({d:+.1f}%)\"\n\nfor name in all_names:\n    short = name.replace(\"BenchmarkEnableCommand/\", \"\").replace(\"Benchmark\", \"\")\n    if name in old and name in new:\n        o_ms, o_b, o_a, o_n = old[name]\n        n_ms, n_b, n_a, n_n = new[name]\n        d_ms = ((n_ms - Install with: go install golang.org/x/perf/cmd/benchstat@latest\"\n  exit 1\nfi\n\ncurrent_branch=$(git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD)\nif [ \"$current_branch\" = \"$BASE_REF\" ]; then\n  echo \"Already on $BASE_REF — nothing to compare. Showing current branch only:\"\n  git checkout \"$current_branch\" --quiet 2>/dev/null\n  on_base_ref=false\n  [ \"$has_changes\" = true ] && git stash pop --quiet && has_changes=false\n  echo \"\"\n  benchstat \"$new_out\"\n  exit 0\nfi\n\ngit checkout \"$current_branch\" --quiet 2>/dev/null\non_base_ref=false\n[ \"$has_changes\" = true ] && git stash pop --quiet && has_changes=false\n\necho \"\"\nbenchstat \"$old_out\" \"$new_out\"\n\"\"\""

> AGENT

The `\s` in the grep pattern is being interpreted as an escape by TOML. Let me use `[[:space:]]` instead:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01L96YzVUqfpvJxmNabypeg3
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/evisdrenova/code/entire/devenv/cli/mise.toml",
  "old_string": "    | grep -E '^(Benchmark.*\\s+[0-9]|goos:|goarch:|pkg:|cpu:)' > \"$out\"",
  "new_string": "    | grep -E '^(Benchmark.*[[:space:]]+[0-9]|goos:|goarch:|pkg:|cpu:)' > \"$out\""
}
```

> AGENT

Done. **20 insertions, 101 deletions.** The new `bench:compare`:

- Uses `benchstat` (Go's standard tool) instead of custom Python — proper statistical aggregation with confidence intervals and p-values
- Keeps the safe cleanup trap (restores branch + stash on interrupt)
- Filters `go test` output with `grep -E '^(Benchmark.*[[:space:]]+[0-9]|goos:|goarch:|pkg:|cpu:)'` to exclude stderr noise
- Falls back to single-file benchstat when the benchmark doesn't exist on the base branch
- Checks for `benchstat` upfront with a clear install instruction

   167→    | grep -E '^(Benchmark.*[[:space:]]+[0-9]|goos:|goarch:|pkg:|cpu:)' > "$out"
   168→  local n
   169→  n=$(grep -c '^Benchmark' "$out" || true)
   170→  if [ "$n" -eq 0 ]; then
   171→    echo "  ERROR: no benchmark results captured."
   172→    return 1
   173→  fi
   174→  echo "  captured $n result lines"
   175→}
   176→
   177→# Run on current branch
   178→new_out="$tmpdir/new.txt"
   179→if ! run_bench "$current_branch" "$new_out"; then
   180→  exit 1
   181→fi
   182→
   183→# Stash, switch to base, run, switch back
   184→if ! git diff --quiet || ! git diff --cached --quiet; then
   185→  has_changes=true
   186→  git stash push -q -m "bench:compare auto-stash"
   187→fi
   188→
```

Hmm, benchstat still shows the full file paths. It uses the full path, not just the basename. I need to use `-col .name` to override column naming, but that requires a different input format.
